## Codex — 2026-10-23T21:37:35.007Z

**The architecture is reasonable; the delegation process is heavier than it needs to be. Several tasks are also too difficult as beginner starting assignments.** I over-specified that part of the plan.

The main weakness is treating “small function” as equivalent to “easy task.” Decimal parsing, overflow handling, calendar arithmetic, and lifecycle precedence can fit into short functions while still requiring considerable understanding.

**The task difficulty is uneven.**

| Difficulty | Tasks | Assessment |
|---|---|---|
| Good starting tasks | F01 name input, F02 optional contact, F03 selection toggle, F10 capacity predicate, F12 role filter, F20 unread count | Suitable with supplied types, examples, and tests. |
| Suitable with guidance | F04 workspace selection, F08 pending availability edits, F09 search matching, F11 sorting campsites, F13 window validation, F15 available-night intersection, F17 reservation classification, F18/F19 ordering | Provide a decision table or collection-processing example. Do not require knowledge of Kotlin idioms. |
| Keep with you initially | F05 positive-count parsing, F06 price parsing, F07 calendar cells, F14 lottery lifecycle, F16 stay validation | Too many edge cases or too much important application meaning for the first assignments. Simplify or revisit after seeing actual submissions. |

The clearest imbalance is [Yan’s proposed bundle](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/docs/part3_implementation_plan.md:122): three of its four tasks fall into the hardest category. Giving everyone four functions does not produce comparable workloads.

**I would simplify five parts of the plan.**

**1. Treat the 20 functions as candidates, not a quota.**

Start with **one easy function per person**. Assess whether they can run tests, understand failures, implement the contract, and open a PR. Release subsequent tasks based on that evidence.

There is no reason to commit upfront to four functions each. Additional small tasks can emerge naturally as the screens are implemented.

**2. Make their instructions much smaller than your implementation plan.**

Each assignment should be understandable without reading this document or the application architecture:

```text
Purpose
The screen needs this function to count unread notifications.

Change
One specified function body.

Inputs and output
Explicit types and behavior.

Examples
Concrete inputs and expected outputs.

Steps
Open file → run tests → implement → rerun → submit PR.

Done
Tests pass, inputs remain unchanged, KDoc is present.
```

Provide the signature, imports, result types, and fixtures. Accept ordinary `if` statements and loops. They should not have to discover `fold`, comparator composition, collection variance, or other unfamiliar Kotlin features to finish.

**3. Keep coverage tooling out of their problem space.**

Keep your **100% function coverage target**, but you configure and verify it centrally. Their normal workflow should be one test command and readable assertion failures.

The proposed per-function registry, special script, ID-based test names, and coverage-report workflow add infrastructure before the first assignment. A direct Gradle test filter and descriptive test class names are enough initially. Coverage can remain an additional automated check.

Also provide Android Studio and Gradle-wrapper instructions. The current assignment example assumes Nix, while the repository already supports other development setups.

**4. Avoid creating a large tree of dependent branches.**

The [branch strategy](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/docs/part3_implementation_plan.md:48) protects green CI, which is necessary, but twenty prepared task branches plus feature integration branches would create significant coordination work.

Prepare a small active batch. Put shared types on a green base first. Keep function PRs independent wherever possible, and integrate each feature promptly once its required functions pass.

Neither previews nor production should need a parallel “temporary correct implementation” of the same function.

**5. Make the schedule less precise and more credible.**

The [daily schedule](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/docs/part3_implementation_plan.md:63) packs startup, identity, navigation, theme, components, fakes, and tests into one day, followed by calendars, images, maps, and alerts the next. That is too much to treat as a dependable estimate.

Keep the milestone targets, but recognize that preparing tests, helping with setup, and reviewing PRs consume your time too. Beginner assignments should not be prerequisites for your next day’s critical work.

For the first assignment, allow setup and learning time rather than assuming a one-day turnaround means one short coding session.

**What I would retain**

- The single-module architecture and existing repository boundaries.
- State-and-callback screens with constructor-injected controllers.
- Pure, deterministic delegated functions.
- Explicit contracts and tests prepared beforehand.
- Separate unit, Compose UI, and Firebase verification.
- Preserving working code and keeping mandatory CI meaningful.

**My recommended revision is to keep the technical plan, shorten the execution machinery, retain the five hardest functions yourself, and begin with five easy assignments.** Grow the task pool after the first submissions show what support each person actually needs.

## Codex — 2026-10-23T22:26:06.119Z

Start with **test support, the shared UI shell, and session bootstrap**. These have no dependency on the five proposed teammate functions.

I would work through this order:

| Order | Work you can complete independently | Main files / responsibilities |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | **Testing foundation** | Add `MainDispatcherRule`, shared `MutableClock`, repository fakes, realistic fixtures, and central coverage configuration. Get one Compose interaction test running on the self-hosted environment. |
| 2 | **Theme and ordinary components** | Implement `Color.kt`, `Type.kt`, `Theme.kt`, `Spacing.kt`; then `AppScreen`, `StepScaffold`, submitting actions, field errors, loading/empty/retry content, confirmation dialogs, and status labels. Use Material 3 directly where sufficient. |
| 3 | **Application bootstrap and dependency wiring** | Implement `CampsbridgeApplication`, `AppContainerFactory`, ViewModel factories, `IdentityProvider`, `FirebaseIdentityProvider`, `LocalAppPreferences`, `AppViewModel`, and `SessionState`. Test initialization, restoration, retry, and interrupted onboarding. |
| 4 | **Navigation and workspace shell** | Implement `AppDestination`, `AppNavigation`, `AppRoot`, and `WorkspaceScaffold`. Establish wizard scoping, Back behavior, tab state, and ID-based destination arguments. Add destinations as their screens become available. |
| 5 | **Shared image handling** | Implement the Photo Picker adapter, content reader, `ImageSlotController`, image state, and local/remote image presentation. Cover selection cancellation, upload failure, replacement/removal, and uncertain publication. |
| 6 | **Calendar and other lead-owned rules** | Implement `CampsiteCalendar`, selection/state types, month cells, date conversions, positive-count parsing, and exact price parsing. These include the explicitly lead-owned F05/F06/F07 functions. Add map display/location selection when campsite setup needs them. |
| 7 | **Required repository extensions** | Add stable draft-ID creation, published campsite-associated lottery lookup, owner reservation observation, live reservation/payment observation, and scoped persistence work. Implement each alongside its first consumer and emulator tests. |
| 8 | **Previewable feature content** | Build onboarding steps, profile forms, campsite setup, discovery, and other screen content using supplied state and callbacks. Their layout, accessibility, navigation callbacks, and Compose tests can progress independently of the small delegated functions. |

The boundaries remain straightforward:

```text
Screen → callbacks → ViewModel → repository interface → data implementation
Route  → supplies ViewModel, collects state, connects navigation
```

Shared components receive values and callbacks. They do not construct repositories, acquire ViewModels, or decide booking eligibility.

Keep these five proposed first tasks available for teammates:

- F01: display-name validation.
- F02: optional-contact normalization.
- F03: selection toggling.
- F10: guest-capacity matching.
- F20: unread receipt counting.

You can prepare their signatures, result types, tests, and call sites while building the surrounding feature. Screen previews use supplied state; the affected production action waits for its implementation. Keep initially failing tests on task branches, and avoid temporary production implementations of those functions.

**The first concrete deliverable should be a small running foundation:** the approved theme, shared screen/step layouts, a debug component gallery, passing test examples, and real identity bootstrap that reaches setup or restores an existing account. Then build the first onboarding/profile content against it.

That gives you useful, reviewable progress immediately. Extend the shared components through actual screens rather than trying to finish an entire component library first.

## Codex — 2026-10-23T23:02:45.831Z

Saved the detailed plan in [part3_foundation_plan.md](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/doc/part3_foundation_plan.md), linked from the main plan.

This covers the **first five independent work packages** from the previous outline. We’ll implement them on `part3/justin` in this order.

1. **Test support and verification**

   Establish a passing baseline, then add coroutine testing and compatible coverage tooling. Verify that coverage measures actual source lines and branches before using it for teammate acceptance.

   Shared fakes and `MutableClock` belong in `src/debug`, making them available to unit tests, instrumented tests, and demo hosts. JVM-specific helpers such as `MainDispatcherRule` remain in `src/test`.

   Add a reusable `TestAppHost` and a self-hosted Android UI job. Keep ordinary unit tests, Firebase contracts, and instrumented tests distinct.

   **Completion:** deterministic controller tests, a meaningful Compose interaction test, inspectable coverage reports, and required CI checks passing.

2. **Shared theme and components**

   Replace the starter theme with the approved white/green palette, Inter typography, consistent spacing/shapes, and dynamic colors disabled. Respect system scaling and existing account accessibility settings.

   Implement:

   - `AppScreen`: app bar, insets, gutters, snackbar and optional footer.
   - `StepScaffold`: progress, short content, Next/Back placement.
   - Shared submission feedback, field errors and action arrangements.
   - Loading, empty, failure/retry and confirmation content.
   - Accessible status labels.

   Use Material 3 directly when a wrapper adds no useful behavior. Add realistic previews and a debug component gallery.

   **Completion:** reusable components work with supplied state/callbacks, including keyboard, long-text, enlarged-font and failure states.

3. **Bootstrap, identity and dependency wiring**

   Extend the existing `AppContainer`; add explicit environment configuration, lazy container construction and typed ViewModel factories.

   Put anonymous Firebase Auth behind `IdentityProvider`. Put account-keyed setup progress and workspace preference behind `LocalAppPreferences`, implemented using [Preferences DataStore](https://developer.android.com/topic/libraries/architecture/datastore).

   `AppViewModel` owns these transitions:

   ```text
   Initialize → establish/restore identity → read account/setup progress
                                         → setup required
                                         → ready
                                         → unavailable
                                         → failure with retry
   ```

   Profile existence alone must not mean onboarding finished. Bootstrap does not create a sample profile or bypass name validation.

   **Completion:** real identity restoration, correct startup decisions, retry handling and cancelled obsolete observations. Tests cover interrupted setup and account/role changes.

4. **Root presentation and navigation**

   `MainActivity` hosts Compose. `AppRoot` renders session state. `AppNavigation` connects implemented feature routes. `WorkspaceScaffold` supplies consistent workspace presentation.

   Initially, an existing account can reach a read-only profile. First-time profile setup remains an explicit pending boundary until its reserved function is implemented. Unimplemented features do not get clickable dead tabs.

   Establish ID-based arguments, actual-caller Back behavior and route-level lifecycle collection. Wizard scoping is added with the first actual wizard.

   **Completion:** startup and existing-account navigation work, with controller and navigation tests using injected dependencies.

5. **Shared image handling**

   Use Android’s [Photo Picker](https://developer.android.com/training/data-storage/shared/photo-picker) and [Coil](https://coil-kt.github.io/coil/network/) for image presentation.

   Separate responsibilities:

   ```text
   Route-owned picker
       → platform content reader
       → local selection/preview
       → explicit save
       → ImageSlotController
       → existing ImageRepository
       → confirmed attachment and remote display
   ```

   `ImageSlotController` is a plain Kotlin helper owned by its parent ViewModel. It receives the repository, a stable image target and a coroutine scope.

   Cover cancellation, replacement/removal, failed URL loading, repeated actions and uncertain publication. Keep a successfully published image visible when obsolete-object cleanup is pending. Display indeterminate upload progress because the current repository provides no measured percentage.

   Integrate this into the read-only profile without implementing name/contact editing.

   **Completion:** existing accounts can select, save, replace and remove their photo, with controller tests, picker/Compose tests and real persistence verification.

The resulting production layout would be:

```text
campsbridge/
├── CampsbridgeApplication.kt
├── MainActivity.kt
├── domain/                         Existing models/contracts
├── data/                           Existing repository implementations
├── di/
│   ├── AppContainer.kt
│   ├── AppContainerFactory.kt
│   ├── AppEnvironment.kt
│   └── ViewModelFactories.kt
├── platform/
│   ├── identity/
```

## Codex — 2026-10-23T23:38:42.731Z

Agreed. **All implementations, tests, and fixtures within these five packages will be lead-owned.** There will be no teammate gaps in this batch.

I recorded the proposed acceptance cases in [part3_foundation_test_plan.md](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/doc/part3_foundation_test_plan.md).

Our strategy is **contract-first TDD**:

1. Define the initial state, action, expected outcome, and permitted side effects.
2. Write an executable test that fails for the missing behavior.
3. Implement the behavior.
4. Refactor while preserving the assertions.
5. Accept the change only when its applicable checks pass.

The tests specify observable behavior, leaving implementation details flexible.

| Package | Cases we establish before implementation |
| --- | --- |
| **Test infrastructure** | Controlled asynchronous execution, reliable teardown, real coverage reports, and meaningful unit/UI tests running through CI. |
| **Shared components** | Enabled/disabled actions, field callbacks, accessible errors, submitting states, confirmation/cancellation, and reachable controls with keyboards/enlarged text. |
| **Bootstrap and identity** | Existing/new identity, initialization failure/retry, missing/removed profiles, interrupted onboarding, valid workspace selection, duplicate initialization, and cancellation. |
| **Navigation and profile** | Correct root destination, live profile updates, unavailable accounts, no duplicate navigation on recomposition, and no profile writes from read-only presentation. |
| **Images** | Selection without upload, explicit save/remove, duplicate actions, failures/retry, stale URL results, uncertain publication, deferred cleanup, and cancellation. |

For ViewModels and controllers, test the **actual production class** with controllable fake repositories and platform interfaces. Use existing JUnit conventions, `runTest`, a shared test scheduler, and deferred results to pause operations. No real-time sleeps or network dependencies in these tests. Android’s [coroutine](https://developer.android.com/kotlin/coroutines/test) and [Flow testing guidance](https://developer.android.com/kotlin/flow/test) supports this approach.

For example, the image save contract becomes several explicit tests:

```text
Given:
  An existing published photo
  A newly selected candidate
  A repository upload held in progress

When:
  Save is pressed twice

Expect:
  Exactly one replacement operation
  Submitting state
  No confirmed-save result yet

When:
  Replacement succeeds

Expect:
  The returned attachment becomes confirmed
  The saved candidate is cleared
  Submitting ends
```

Separate cases cover failure preserving the candidate, successful publication with cleanup pending, and uncertain publication requiring reconciliation before another replacement.

We’ll keep three kinds of automated evidence distinct:

- **JVM tests:** decisions, state transitions, retries, cancellation, and side effects.
- **Compose/intent tests:** actual controls, semantics, navigation wiring, and picker results. These verify that the UI connects to the tested behavior. [Compose testing guidance](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/testing).
- **Firebase Emulator tests:** actual identity, queries, uploaded bytes, attachment publication, and cleanup contracts.

For coverage, I propose **100% executable line and source-branch coverage for new small pure helpers**. Controllers must pass their explicit behavior matrix, with uncovered source decisions reviewed and missing cases added. We won’t impose an artificial project-wide 100% JVM threshold on Compose and SDK code.

We also won’t test private methods, every incidental `StateFlow` emission, or every Material color constant. Those assertions make refactoring expensive without proving useful behavior.

The contract document contains the detailed cases per test suite. These are proposed acceptance tests; implementation has not started.

## Codex — 2026-10-23T23:56:31.730Z

For these five packages, **we own both tests and implementation**, with no teammate placeholders. Tests should define the agreed behavior before we implement it.

Use three complementary layers:

- **JVM unit tests:** rules, ViewModels, and controllers. Fast, deterministic, no Android device or network.
- **Compose/platform tests:** user interactions, navigation wiring, accessibility semantics, and Android integrations.
- **Firebase emulator tests:** actual persistence, queries, transactions, and Storage behavior. Preserve the existing suite.

The proposed coverage is:

| Package | Contracts to establish beforehand |
|---|---|
| Test infrastructure | Controlled coroutine execution, working coverage reports, meaningful instrumented interaction, mandatory CI checks |
| Shared theme/components | Callbacks, disabled/busy actions, visible errors, confirmation/cancellation, scrolling and accessibility scaling |
| Bootstrap and dependencies | Identity restoration, startup states, missing/removed accounts, interrupted setup, failure/retry, valid workspace selection |
| Root/navigation/profile | Correct root branch, accurate profile data, account updates, unavailable-account handling, no duplicate navigation |
| Image handling | Selection versus saving, cancellation, upload/remove outcomes, retry, repeated taps, stale results, uncertain publication |

**For each behavior, follow this sequence:**

1. Write a short Given/When/Then acceptance case.
2. Write a compiling test with the smallest necessary production interface.
3. Confirm it fails because the behavior is missing.
4. Implement the behavior.
5. Refactor while keeping the assertions unchanged.

Once the tests are reviewed and mature, we treat them as the contract. We change them only when an agreed requirement changes or a test is demonstrably incorrect. We should avoid adding incidental tests simply to match how the implementation happens to work.

For ViewModels and controllers, test the **real production class**, replacing only dependencies with small handwritten fakes. Control time, coroutine scheduling, repository responses, and pending operations. For example:

```text
Given: a selected photo and a repository upload held pending
When:  Save is pressed twice
Then:  one upload starts, submitting is visible,
       and another mutation cannot start

When:  that upload fails
Then:  the candidate remains available for explicit retry
```

Coverage should support these contracts:

- **Small deterministic helpers:** 100% executable line and branch coverage.
- **ViewModels/controllers:** cover the agreed state transitions, errors, cancellation, and important side effects; inspect uncovered decisions.
- **Compose:** test interactions and accessibility, supplemented by visual review. Avoid pixel-perfect snapshots or assertions for every color constant.

Before accepting each package, its focused tests and applicable existing checks must pass. No ignored failing tests or weakened CI gates.

The detailed proposed cases are already captured in [the foundation test plan](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/doc/part3_foundation_test_plan.md).

## Codex — 2026-10-24T06:16:20.312Z

For these five packages, **we own both the tests and the implementation, with no teammate gaps**. I would use the existing [foundation test plan](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/doc/part3_foundation_test_plan.md) as the acceptance contract.

The strategy is to agree on observable behavior first, write executable tests, then implement against those expectations.

**Separate the tests by what they prove.**

| Layer | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| JVM unit tests | Decisions, ViewModel/controller state, failures, cancellation, retries, and duplicate-operation prevention. Use real production classes with fake dependencies. |
| Android adapter tests | Identity adapter, persistent local preferences, and selected-image content access. Use controlled SDK outcomes or temporary files; Robolectric where needed. |
| Compose and intent tests | Controls invoke the right actions, states appear correctly, navigation connects, and photo-picker results reach the application. |
| Firebase integration tests | Actual repository reads, writes, uploads, and coordinated operations against disposable emulators. Preserve the existing suite. |

Compose tests exercise UI behavior through semantics and user actions. They complement the controller tests. [Android Compose testing](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/testing)

**The agreed cases for each package would be:**

| Package | Explicit acceptance cases |
|---|---|
| **1. Test infrastructure** | Demonstrate a real controller operation held in progress and completed deterministically. Run a meaningful shared-button interaction. Generate coverage from actual production code. Keep Firebase tests separate from ordinary unit tests. Required CI suites must fail when their prerequisites are unavailable. |
| **2. Theme and shared components** | Enabled actions invoke callbacks; disabled/submitting actions do not. Fields report edits and accessible errors. Retry, confirmation, cancellation, and dismissal invoke the correct callbacks. Recomposition causes no writes. Loading, empty, error, and content remain distinct. Actions stay reachable with long content, keyboard, and enlarged text. App scaling is applied once while respecting system scaling. |
| **3. Bootstrap and dependencies** | Restore an existing identity; create one when absent; report authentication failure. Concurrent initialization does not create competing sessions. Missing profile leads to setup; interrupted setup stays interrupted; an existing active profile can become ready. Failed reads never become guessed success. Workspace selection respects actual account capabilities and persists only confirmed changes. Retry works, and cancelled or obsolete work cannot overwrite current state. |
| **4. Root presentation and navigation** | Each session state renders its matching screen. Existing profile data appears accurately. Profile updates refresh the UI. Recomposition does not duplicate destinations or restart authentication. Unavailable accounts lose access to profile actions. Every displayed navigation action has an implemented destination. |
| **5. Shared image handling** | Selecting a photo previews it without uploading. Picker cancellation preserves existing selection. Explicit Save performs one replacement; repeated taps cannot duplicate it. Success displays the confirmed attachment. Failure retains the candidate for retry. Removal requires confirmation and reports actual results. Display failures do not trigger uploads. Stale results cannot replace newer selections. Uncertain publication is reconciled before another replacement. Streams are opened lazily and correctly consumed/closed. |

We test only implemented navigation and features. Full onboarding, future tabs, and notification deep links receive contracts when those features enter scope.

**Each test should specify initial state, action, and observable outcome.**

For example:

```text
Given:
  An existing profile photo.
  A newly selected replacement.
  The replacement operation has not completed.

When:
  Save is pressed twice.

Then:
  Exactly one replacement request is made.
  Submission remains visible.
  Further mutations are disabled.
  Success is not reported prematurely.

When:
  The replacement fails.

Then:
  Submission ends.
  The selected replacement remains available.
  The existing confirmed photo remains unchanged.
  An explicit retry is available.
```

That is precise enough to settle before implementation while allowing us to refactor internal code freely.

**Keep asynchronous tests deterministic.**

Use `runTest`, one shared test scheduler, controlled Flow values, and deferred fake results. Advance execution deliberately; avoid sleeps, public network services, and arbitrary timeouts. Clear ViewModels and cancel collectors after each test. This follows Android’s coroutine-testing guidance. [Coroutine testing](https://developer.android.com/kotlin/coroutines/test)

**Coverage should support the contracts.**

- Require **100% executable line and branch coverage for small pure helpers**, backed by meaningful assertions.
- For ViewModels/controllers, require every agreed success, failure, boundary, and concurrency case; inspect uncovered decisions.
- Verify Compose through interaction tests and review typography/layout on a device.
- Keep actual persistence verification in the [Firebase Emulator Suite](https://firebase.google.com/docs/emulator-suite).

Avoid an app-wide 100% target, pixel-perfect screenshot baselines, or tests that merely repeat color constants.

For each new behavior: **agree on the contract → write a failing test → implement → pass → refactor with the same assertions**. Once agreed, expectations stay stable unless we explicitly identify a mistaken requirement. Existing working behavior stays intact, and every mergeable checkpoint must pass its required checks.

## Codex — 2026-10-24T15:13:06.150Z

**Next: complete the account flow, starting with onboarding.** The five foundation packages are finished; a fresh installation currently stops at the setup placeholder. This is the next gap in [M1 of our implementation plan](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/doc/part3_implementation_plan.md:193).

The next usable workflow should be:

```text
Fresh installation
  → Welcome
  → Find campsites / Start hosting
  → Name
  → Optional interests / Owner introduction
  → Confirmed account and selected workspace
  → Profile

Reopen → restore the same account and workspace
```

I would proceed in this order:

1. **Agree on onboarding test contracts.** Cover branching, Back preserving input, invalid names, optional-step skipping, failed saves, repeated submission, interrupted owner activation, and restart. An account appearing in Firestore must not prematurely finish onboarding.

2. **Build the onboarding UI and wiring.** Use the approved designs and existing shared components. One `OnboardingViewModel` owns the draft and progression; individual steps receive state and callbacks. Reuse the existing account repository and local progress storage.

3. **Prepare the first small teammate tasks.** F01, display-name validation, now has a real onboarding consumer. F02, optional-contact normalization, follows with profile editing. Provide the exact contracts, failing tests, permitted files, completed example, and test commands. Keep unfinished implementations on task branches while the shared base stays green.

4. **Finish the remaining M1 screens.** Add personal/contact editing, the settings entry, and policy pages. Photo handling is already available for reuse.

Then move into **M2: owner creates and publishes a campsite → another account discovers and inspects it**, followed by the lottery, result Inbox, and booking workflow.

Make the foundation available through a PR so teammates can start from the tested base. **Our immediate coding step should be the onboarding test contract and screen-by-screen implementation proposal.**

Files: [OnboardingViewModel.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/onboarding/OnboardingViewModel.kt)

## Codex — 2026-10-24T15:16:36.925Z

Yes. **Our agreed sequence is to finish items 1–8 independently before relying on teammate implementations.** My previous answer moved into delegation too early.

Items **1–5 are complete**. We should continue with:

| Next | Work | Boundary |
|---|---|---|
| **6** | Calendar, date conversions, count and price parsing | Fully implemented and tested by you, including F05/F06/F07. |
| **7** | Required repository extensions | Lead-owned. Add each alongside its first consumer and emulator tests. |
| **8** | Previewable feature screens | Build layouts and interactions using supplied state, callbacks, and fixtures. Reserved teammate functions remain outside preview dependencies. |

**The immediate next batch should be item 6:**

- `PositiveCountInput.kt`: positive whole-number parsing, maximum limits, and overflow handling.
- `PriceInput.kt`: exact conversion from entered CAD amounts to integer cents.
- `MonthCells.kt`: calendar dates and padding for the chosen first weekday.
- `DateInputs.kt`: conversions between Material picker values and campsite-local dates.
- `CampsiteCalendar.kt` and small state/selection types: month navigation, selected dates, and unavailable/booked-date presentation. Persistence stays outside the component.

We should settle the test cases first: invalid and boundary inputs, monetary overflow, leap years and month layouts, timezone-safe date round trips, and calendar selection/navigation. Then implement against those tests and review before committing.

After that, items **7 and 8 can progress together**: build previewable screens, identify their concrete repository needs, and implement those extensions. Maps enter with campsite setup.

Throughout this phase, F01/F02/F03/F10/F20 remain available for later teammate assignments. We can finish the surrounding UI, wiring, fixtures, and tests without implementing those reserved functions or presenting unfinished operations as successful.

## Codex — 2026-10-24T15:21:32.784Z

**Items 6–8 remain work you can complete independently.** We keep the existing single app module, reuse the completed foundation, and preserve the small functions reserved for teammates.

**For item 6, finish the shared calendar and lead-owned input rules.**

| Files | What to implement |
|---|---|
| `ui/common/input/PositiveCountInput.kt` | **F05:** trim input, parse positive whole numbers, enforce the supplied maximum, and return clear errors for blank, invalid, or overflowing input. |
| `ui/common/input/PriceInput.kt` | **F06:** parse nonnegative CAD amounts directly into `Long` cents. Handle decimal precision and overflow without floating-point arithmetic. |
| `ui/components/calendar/MonthCells.kt` | **F07:** generate the month’s dates, padded into complete weeks according to the supplied first weekday. |
| `ui/common/DateInputs.kt` | Convert Material date-picker values to/from `LocalDate`. Keep picker UTC conventions separate from campsite timezone conversions. |
| `ui/components/calendar/CalendarState.kt`, `CalendarSelection.kt` | Small presentation types for the displayed month, date availability, selection, and loading/error states. |
| `ui/components/calendar/CampsiteCalendar.kt` | Shared calendar presentation: month navigation, weekday labels, selected dates, unavailable/booked dates, and accessible descriptions. Compose Material controls; keep persistence outside it. |
| `ui/common/format/DisplayFormats.kt` | Shared date, stay-date, and CAD display formatting where actual screens need it. |
| `platform/maps/CampsiteMap.kt`, `LocationPicker.kt` | Add stored-location display and coordinate selection when the campsite location step becomes a consumer. |

The calendar receives state and emits callbacks. Its callers own requested changes, booking validation, and saving.

Agree on tests before implementation:

- Parsing: blank, malformed, exact limits, leading zeros, fractional prices, and overflow.
- Month cells: different starting weekdays, leap years, century boundaries, and four/five/six-week layouts.
- Date conversions: round trips and timezone boundaries.
- Calendar interactions: month changes, selection callbacks, unavailable dates, loading/error presentation, enlarged text, and accessibility.

Require full executable line/branch coverage for the small pure helpers. Use Compose interaction tests for the calendar.

**For item 7, implement the missing repository capabilities and their coordination.**

These extend existing repositories and models.

| Extension | Responsibility and first consumer |
|---|---|
| Stable campsite draft IDs | Allow creation with a caller-supplied ID. Retrying the same draft returns the existing record without overwriting confirmed changes. Used by campsite setup. |
| Stable lottery draft IDs | Apply the same creation/retry contract to lottery setup. |
| Published lotteries for a campsite | Add `observePublishedForCampsite(campsiteId)`. Resolve the existing lottery/offer relationship inside the repository. Used by campsite details. |
| Owner reservation observation | Add `observeForOwner(ownerId)`, deriving ownership through campsites. Used by scoped work and the owner’s reservation list. |
| Live reservation/payment observation | Add `observe(id)` and `observePayment(id)`. Used by confirmation, reservation details, cancellation, and refund presentation. |
| Scoped persistence work | Add `PendingWork`, `SessionPendingWork`, and `ForegroundWorkRunner`. Resume relevant draws and notification delivery for the active account; coordinate foreground/deadline triggers, cancellation, and retries. |
| Cancellation date guard | Add the agreed checkout-date eligibility rule alongside cancellation/rejection integration. Preserve idempotent retries of already-cancelled reservations. |

Keep Firestore queries, relationship assembly, transactions, and listener cleanup in `data/`. Domain interfaces expose meaningful operations and `Flow`s. Platform code owns lifecycle scheduling.

Emulator tests should establish:

- Stable-ID retries and concurrent creation produce one draft.
- Existing confirmed data survives retries.
- Queries update correctly as related records change.
- Subscriptions are released when observation ends.
- Scoped processing resumes unfinished work without duplicating completed outcomes.
- Cancellation boundaries use the reservation’s recorded timezone.

Implement each extension with its first concrete consumer and fixture scenarios.

**For item 8, build the previewable feature screens and their independent wiring.**

Build one screen family at a time, following the approved designs:

| Screen family | Content to build |
|---|---|
| Onboarding | Welcome, starting intent, name, optional interests, and owner introduction. One draft/progression owner for the wizard. |
| Profile and policies | Profile menu, personal/contact forms, settings entry, policy menu, and policy reader. Reuse the existing photo controls. |
| Owner campsites | Listing list; setup steps for basics, photo, location, capacity/price, amenities, rules, availability, and publication review. |
| Discovery | Explore/results, filter editor, campsite overview, location, availability, and rules. Reuse campsite presentation across workspaces. |
| Lotteries | Owner/entrant lists, setup steps, detail, participants, processing, and result states. |
| Booking | Stay dates, guests, terms, quote/review, submission, and saved confirmation. |
| Inbox and reservations | Notification rows, active reservation lists, reservation/payment details, accepted terms, and cancellation/rejection confirmations. |
| Administration | Read-only campsite, profile, image, and notification-log browsing and inspection. |

For each family:

- Implement stateless screen content receiving state and callbacks.
- Add realistic fixtures, themed previews, and explicit debug demonstrations.
- Cover loading, empty, failure, submission, long text, and enlarged-font states.
- Add Compose tests for interactions and semantics.
- Add routes and ViewModels where their independent dependencies are ready.
- Keep production saves and navigation tied to actual implemented behavior.

Reserved teammate functions remain pending. Preview hosts supply fixture state and callbacks, so their bodies are unnecessary for building and testing layouts. Their unfinished contract tests stay on task branches, keeping the shared base green.

**Implementation sequence:** finish item 6, then progress through item 8 by screen family, introducing item 7’s repository extensions as each family needs them. This completes substantial UI and backend work before teammate integration becomes necessary.

## Codex — 2026-10-24T15:32:30.325Z

I’d treat **6 as a shared UI foundation** and **7 as a persistence refactor**. Keep the single `:app` module and existing package boundaries.

Since there is no stored data, we can change contracts and documents directly, update every consumer and fixture, and remove obsolete APIs. There is no reason to introduce compatibility branches.

**6. Calendar, dates, and input rules**

I would organize it as:

```text
ui/
├── components/calendar/
│   ├── CampsiteCalendar.kt
│   ├── CalendarState.kt
│   ├── CalendarSelection.kt
│   └── MonthCells.kt
└── common/
    ├── DateInputs.kt
    └── input/
        ├── PositiveCountInput.kt
        └── PriceInput.kt
```

The boundaries would be:

| Part | Responsibility |
|---|---|
| `MonthCells` | Calculate the dates and padding for a month. Pure Kotlin. |
| `CalendarState` | Describe the displayed month and each day’s presentation: available, unavailable, occupied, selected. |
| `CalendarSelection` | Represent selected dates or a stay range. |
| `CampsiteCalendar` | Render supplied state and emit month/date callbacks. |
| `DateInputs` | Translate Material date-picker values into application dates. |
| Input parsers | Turn incomplete text into a typed value or an explicit validation error. |

The calendar would receive state and callbacks, following our existing screen conventions. It would not fetch inventory, decide booking eligibility, or save availability.

Selection and inventory would remain separate: selecting an occupied date must never change its underlying availability. A future ViewModel owns the editable selection and confirmed repository state.

Use ordinary Compose layout and Material controls for the inventory calendar. Material date pickers can handle ordinary date selection; our campsite calendar adds the per-night availability presentation.

Stay dates remain `LocalDate`. Material picker milliseconds are an encoding converted at the UI boundary; they must not shift a campsite night according to the phone’s timezone.

For the parsers, retain the agreed contracts:

- `parsePositiveCount(text, maximum)` accepts whole positive counts within the limit and returns explicit errors for user input.
- `parsePriceCents(text)` parses directly into integer cents, including overflow checks.
- `buildMonthCells(month, firstWeekday)` produces dates padded to complete weeks.

I would defer map/location-picker integration until campsite setup provides its first consumer.

**7. Repository contracts and persistence structure**

There are five changes I would make.

**A. Make draft identity part of creation**

Replace the existing generated-ID creation contract with required caller-supplied identity:

```kotlin
suspend fun createDraft(
    id: String,
    ownerId: String,
    details: CampsiteDetails,
): Campsite
```

Apply the same pattern to lotteries.

The transaction contract would be:

- Missing document: create the draft.
- Existing document belonging to the same owner: return the stored record unchanged.
- Existing document belonging to another owner: report a conflict.

Returning the stored record matters: after an uncertain acknowledgement, the caller receives confirmed state rather than assuming its attempted values were saved.

The future wizard allocates its ID once and retains it in saved state. Subsequent edits use `update`. This makes retries safe without an additional idempotency collection.

**B. Give campsite-to-lottery lookup an explicit query model**

Currently, an offer stores only its nights; the campsite ID exists in its path. That makes reverse lookup awkward.

I would retain the existing authoritative relationship:

```text
lotteries/{lotteryId}/offers/{campsiteId}
```

and add one query field:

```text
campsiteId: String
nights: List<Date>
```

Then `observePublishedForCampsite(campsiteId)` can:

1. Observe matching offer documents.
2. Resolve the corresponding published lottery records.
3. Update its results when either relationships or lottery records change.

This is a deliberate query copy of the path ID. The codec and writes must enforce equality with that path; offer membership still has one owning document. Firestore supports these collection-group queries with the appropriate index. [Firebase query documentation](https://firebase.google.com/docs/firestore/query-data/queries#collection-group-query)

This also preserves multiple-campsite lotteries. The repository owns the joining and subscription management.

**C. Make simulated payment part of the reservation aggregate**

This is the structural change I would recommend most strongly.

Our payment has one reservation, one simulated charge, and a possible full refund. Booking and cancellation already own all its writes. I would therefore change storage to:

```text
reservations/{reservationId}
  ...existing reservation fields...
  payment: {
    amountCents,
    paidAt,
    refundedAt
  }
```

In Kotlin:

```text
Reservation
  ...
  payment: Payment
```

`Payment` remains a clear value type, without its own reservation ID.

Then the repository exposes:

```kotlin
fun observe(id: String): Flow<Reservation?>
```

That one observation includes reservation, cancellation, and payment state. We can remove the separate `payments` collection and `getPayment` API instead of adding an independent `observePayment` stream.

This makes the aggregate explicit and avoids asking every detail screen to reconcile two independently arriving snapshots.

Booking still transactionally creates the reservation, claims calendar nights, and queues its notification. Cancellation updates the reservation/payment, releases nights, and queues its notification in the same transaction.

**D. Keep owner reservation assembly inside data**

Add:

```kotlin
fun observeForOwner(ownerId: String): Flow<List<Reservation>>
```

Its implementation observes the owner’s campsites and their reservations. Ownership remains authoritative on campsites.

Important contract details:

- Include retained campsite records so removal does not erase reservation history.
- Handle an owner with no campsites.
- Release subscriptions when the observed campsite set changes.
- Return deduplicated results in a documented, stable order.
- Propagate failures rather than presenting an empty list.

For this project’s scale, that is a manageable join and preserves our existing ownership model.

**E. Restructure pending work around its originating record**

The current global recovery scans should not become ordinary startup behavior. I would also revise the earlier idea of recovering notifications by reconstructing known event IDs: that is incomplete for randomly identified announcements and messages.

Instead, place delivery jobs under the operation’s parent:

```text
lotteries/{lotteryId}/_notificationDeliveries/{eventId}

reservations/{reservationId}/_notificationDeliveries/{eventId}
```

Lottery results and announcements belong to the lottery. Reservation confirmations, cancellations, and conversation alerts belong to the reservation.

Event content remains in `notifications/{eventId}`; inbox receipts remain under accounts. These jobs only own pending delivery work.

The runtime ownership would be:

```text
ForegroundWorkRunner
  owns lifecycle and active-account cancellation
          ↓
SessionPendingWork
  discovers relevant records
  schedules due work
  serializes/coalesces processing
          ↓
Lottery draw / notification delivery implementations
  own durable operations and retry behavior
```

`AppContainer` constructs these dependencies; recovery orchestration moves out of it.

The session coordinator would:

- Process the current owner’s due lotteries.
- Observe delivery work for relevant lotteries and reservations.
- Wake at deadlines and when relevant persisted work changes.
- Run one processing pass at a time.
- Preserve interrupted work for retry.
- Isolate independent failures while propagating coroutine cancellation.

Global maintenance remains an explicit administrative operation. Existing resumable draw behavior and image/removal cleanup must remain working.

The corresponding implementation layout would be approximately:

```text
data/
├── lottery/
│   ├── FirestoreLotteryRepository.kt
│   ├── LotteryQueries.kt
│   └── LotteryDraw.kt
├── booking/
│   ├── FirestoreBookingRepository.kt
│   ├── BookingQueries.kt
│   └── BookingWrites.kt
├── notification/
│   ├── FirestoreNotificationRepository.kt
│   ├── NotificationWrites.kt
│   └── NotificationDelivery.kt
└── maintenance/
    └── SessionPendingWork.kt

domain/maintenance/
└── PendingWork.kt

platform/lifecycle/
└── ForegroundWorkRunner.kt
```

Those internal files separate queries, coordinated writes, and resumable processing. They do not introduce extra public repository layers.

**Testing**

I would agree on these contracts and write their tests before implementation:

| Area | Tests |
|---|---|
| Count and price parsing | Valid input, whitespace, leading zeros, invalid formats, exact limits, overflow, invalid maximum. |
| Month cells | Different first weekdays, leap years, century boundaries, 4/5/6 rows, every date appearing exactly once. |
| Date conversion | Picker round trips across timezones and daylight-saving boundaries without changing stay dates. |
| Calendar Compose interactions | Month navigation, selection callbacks, disabled dates, selected/occupied semantics, enlarged text. |
| Draft creation | Repeated and concurrent same-ID creation produces one record; retry preserves stored edits; conflicting ownership fails. |
| Lottery lookup | Correct campsite matching, multiple-campsite offers, publication changes, offer removal, empty results, listener cleanup. |
| Reservation observation | Creation, cancellation, and refund arrive through the complete reservation record. |
| Owner reservation observation | Multiple sites, unrelated owners, retained sites, changing subscriptions, stable ordering. |
| Booking invariants | Concurrent booking cannot claim the same nights; failed operations leave no partial reservation, payment, or occupancy changes. |
| Delivery recovery | Interrupted fan-out resumes, first-read timestamps survive retries, incomplete draws withhold results, announcements and message alerts are discovered. |
| Foreground coordination | Deadline wakeup, coalesced triggers, no overlapping passes, background/account-switch cancellation, independent failure recovery. |

Use ordinary JVM tests for pure rules and coordination with fakes, Compose tests for the calendar, and Firebase Emulator tests for actual queries and transactions. Exercise the scoped queries using owner and entrant identities, rather than relying solely on administrative access.

Keep the scoped **100% line/branch requirement for the small pure helpers**. Repository correctness comes from observable transaction and recovery scenarios.

I would implement **6 first**, then **draft creation → reservation/payment aggregate → queries → scoped delivery and lifecycle recovery**. Each step would update schema, codecs, rules, fixtures, callers, and tests together. Feature screens remain deferred.

Blocked audit:
- Do not call update_goal with status "blocked" the first time a blocker appears.
- Only use status "blocked" when the same blocking condition has repeated for at least three consecutive goal turns, counting the original/user-triggered turn and any automatic goal continuations.
- If the user resumes a goal that was previously marked "blocked", treat the resumed run as a fresh blocked audit. If the same blocking condition then repeats for at least three consecutive resumed goal turns, call update_goal with status "blocked" again.
- Use status "blocked" only when you are truly at an impasse and cannot make meaningful progress without user input or an external-state change.
- Once the blocked threshold is satisfied, do not keep reporting that you are still blocked while leaving the goal active; call update_goal with status "blocked".
- Never use status "blocked" merely because the work is hard, slow, uncertain, incomplete, or would benefit from clarification.

Call update_goal only after the completion or blocked audit passes, or when the user explicitly requests pausing this goal. For a requested pause, use status "paused", report the returned status, and stop goal work; never pause on your own initiative. Do not mark a goal complete merely because the budget is nearly exhausted or because you are stopping work.

</codex_internal_context>

## Codex — 2026-10-24T17:44:35.918Z

**Next, resume item 8, starting with onboarding and profile.** The shared foundation is ready, but a new installation still reaches the setup placeholder; the main feature screens remain pending.

I would proceed in this order:

1. **Define the onboarding contract and tests.** Settle step transitions, Back behavior, preserved input, optional-step skipping, interrupted setup, failed saves, and owner activation. Keep account capabilities separate from the selected workspace.

2. **Build previewable onboarding content.** Follow the approved designs: Welcome → intent → name → entrant interests or owner introduction. Each step receives state and callbacks, uses shared components, and has normal/error/submitting/enlarged-text previews.

3. **Wire the flow through one ViewModel.** Reuse the existing account repository, local setup progress, navigation, and dependency factories. Finish setup only after the necessary writes succeed; preserve progress across interruption.

4. **Extend profile editing and prepare the first teammate tasks.** Keep F01 display-name parsing and F02 optional-contact normalization reserved. Supply their fixed contracts, initially failing tests, completed examples, exact permitted files, and production consumers before assigning them. Keep unfinished bodies and failing tests on task branches so required CI remains green.

After that, proceed through **campsite setup and discovery → lotteries and Inbox → booking and reservation management**. Add maps when campsite location becomes the active workflow.

The next concrete deliverable should be **an onboarding screen/state outline and test matrix**, followed by its previewable UI. We should also reconcile stale “planned” descriptions in the implementation documents as those sections become active.

See [04.03 HTML](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/prototype/screens/5e0d1a4534a24c02b82e5581ddc41b75.html), [09.02 HTML](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/prototype/screens/16137260834989062632.html), and [08.16 HTML](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/prototype/screens/16137260834989063414.html).

The shared structure is grouped key/value information; actions and whether the data is editable differ. See [04.03 HTML](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/prototype/screens/5e0d1a4534a24c02b82e5581ddc41b75.html), [07.05 HTML](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/prototype/screens/604ae15bc5654495944a3b700b3eb4cb.html), and [08.16 HTML](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/prototype/screens/16137260834989063414.html).

- **Calendar and date/time controls:** `04.06 — E02c Refined availability`, `07.02 — E07b Final choose dates`, `10.08 — O04 Final setup availability`, and `11.03 — O06b1 Allocation` share month grids and month navigation. They represent different operations: read-only availability, entrant date selection, owner availability setup, and lottery-night allocation. `11.04`/`11.05` separately repeat opening/closing date and time fields for registration and booking windows. See [07.02 HTML](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/prototype/screens/4125359717923169419.html), [10.08 HTML](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/prototype/screens/6225082418540336643.html), and [11.04 HTML](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/prototype/screens/5109495634400376718.html).

- **Inbox, message, and review rows:** `13.01 — S05 Final Inbox` has notification summary rows; `13.06 — S05 Inbox Messages` has conversation previews; `13.08 — S05 Owner Inbox clean` lists new messages and announcement navigation. `05.01 — E03 Reviewed mobile reviews` uses author, rating, review text, reactions, and replies; `14.07 — A04 Message logs refined` uses sender/recipient, timestamp, excerpt, and reservation reference. These share a compact, repeated-row composition, but the identity, metadata, and row actions are feature-specific. See [13.01 HTML](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/prototype/screens/3509959953210401951.html), [13.06 HTML](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/prototype/screens/13091609883658690277.html), [05.01 HTML](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/prototype/screens/7605112092733836843.html), and [14.07 HTML](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/prototype/screens/13584749546667201620.html).

- **Bottom navigation and action bars:** Entrant screens show five destinations such as Explore, My lotteries, Reservations, Inbox, and Profile; owner screens use a five-destination set including Campsites and Lotteries; admin screens use Browse, Logs, and Profile. Booking and setup screens instead use bottom step actions such as Back/Next, Apply, or Save/Publish. The destination sets and actions differ even where the docked bar composition repeats. Examples: [04.01 HTML](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/prototype/screens/15363171414317941683.html), [09.01 HTML](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/prototype/screens/16137260834989060975.html), and [07.05 HTML](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/prototype/screens/604ae15bc5654495944a3b700b3eb4cb.html).

**Asset distinction:** The wiring map contains 149 screen routes, and each route points to a unique local HTML asset; none of these routes share one HTML file. These are repeated visual patterns across distinct screens, not aliases of the same screen. `15.01 — Shared controls` is also a distinct route and shows example controls and tokens in [its HTML](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/prototype/screens/18221984054867131374.html).

**Unresolved questions:** Which patterns should become shared controls versus feature-specific templates is a design decision. Calendar reuse also depends on how much interaction and status rendering should be common across read-only, selection, and allocation use cases.

**Recommended next steps:** Use this inventory to select component boundaries, then compare the chosen patterns against `15.01 — Shared controls` and the relevant screen variants. This audit covered only `prototype/wiring.json` and its referenced HTML; it did not inspect implementation code, tests, or dependencies.

## Codex — 2026-10-24T17:51:16.432Z

Yes. The next phase is translating the prototype into native Compose screens. **Before that, I would do one focused shared-controls pass.** The foundation is usable, but several repeated patterns still need to be settled.

I compared the [design conventions](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/doc/screens/DESIGNS.md), [local prototype](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/prototype/wiring.json), implementation plan, and existing Kotlin components.

What is already implemented:

| Area | Current implementation | Remaining limitation |
|---|---|---|
| Theme | White/green palette, Inter, Material typography, spacing, color-blind palette, font/control scaling | Additional compositions need to consistently consume these conventions |
| Screen frame | `AppScreen`: app bar, Back, insets, gutters, snackbar, bottom actions | Needs a settled relationship with workspace navigation and welcome/detail layouts |
| Wizard frame | `StepScaffold`: progress text, Back, Next, submitting state, overflow scrolling | Action text is fixed to Next; no shared segmented progress, Skip, Cancel, or final-step action arrangement |
| Text input | `AppTextField`: single-line input, labels, errors, keyboard configuration | No multiline composition, normal helper text, unit/prefix/suffix slots, or picker-field composition |
| Actions and feedback | Submitting primary button, loading/empty/retry content, confirmation dialog, icon-and-text status | Secondary/destructive action arrangements and richer inline notices need consistent treatment |
| Images | Photo Picker, image controller, upload/retry/removal, Coil display | Image editor presentation is currently a fixed square; list thumbnails, campsite photos, and avatars need suitable presentation |
| Calendar and input rules | Shared calendar, availability/selection states, month navigation, count/price parsers, UTC picker-date conversion | Date/time picker dialogs and schedule fields remain unimplemented |
| Workspace shell | Capability-limited workspace switcher | Currently Profile-specific; no shared bottom navigation |
| Verification | Component gallery, previews, Compose interactions, image/calendar tests | Gallery does not yet demonstrate the remaining repeated patterns |

So we have the basic controls, but the **compositions around them** are incomplete.

The missing elements worth implementing ahead of screens are:

| Shared element | Where the prototype repeats it | Proposed responsibility |
|---|---|---|
| **Navigation and settings rows** | Profile, Settings, campsite sections, reservation details | Consistent title, optional supporting value/icon, chevron, disabled state, and whole-row interaction |
| **Choice rows and chip groups** | Starting intent, interests, preferences, amenities/tags | Single-choice and multiple-choice presentation, selection semantics, wrapping at enlarged text sizes |
| **Bounded count stepper** | Guest filters, booking guests, campsite capacity, winner count, waitlist limit | Supplied value/bounds, increment/decrement callbacks, disabled boundaries, accessible value announcement |
| **Expanded form fields** | Campsite descriptions/rules, announcements, contact details, price | Multiline input, helper/error text, units, read-only picker fields, consistent keyboard behavior |
| **Date/time fields and dialogs** | Discovery dates, registration window, booking window | Temporary picker selection; Confirm applies it, Cancel preserves the prior value; explicit timezone handling |
| **Summary rows and sections** | Listing review, booking confirmation, reservation/payment details | Consistent label/value hierarchy, optional Edit action, long-value wrapping, totals and supporting explanations |
| **Display formatting** | Prices, nightly rates, stay dates, deadlines, guest/night counts | Shared CAD and date formatting; explicit locale/timezone; plural resources |
| **Image presentations** | Campsite cards/details, profile, admin inspection | Consistent aspect ratios, cropping, missing-image and compact failure states, reusing the existing image pipeline |
| **Inline notices** | Failed saves, quote changes, unavailable dates, pending draws, partial saves | Message, semantic emphasis, optional recovery action; preserve surrounding content |
| **Workspace navigation** | Entrant, owner, admin primary pages | Role-specific destinations, selected state, badges, consistent bottom bar |
| **Wizard progress and actions** | Onboarding, campsite setup, lottery setup, booking | Correct current/total progress; configurable continuation label; optional Skip/Cancel; consistent footer placement |

Ordinary date/range and time selection should compose the standard Material controls. Our availability calendar continues to handle inventory-specific presentation. [Date pickers](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/datepickers), [time pickers](https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/components/time-pickers).

There are also repeated **feature components** we can prepare with fixtures before their screens:

- `CampsiteCard` and a compact campsite row.
- `LotteryCard`, shared between owner and entrant layouts where appropriate.
- `ReservationCard` and `StaySummary`.
- A discovery toolbar shared by Explore’s list and map presentations.

These should live beside their owning feature. A campsite card has different information and actions from a reservation card; we should share their image, status, and summary pieces without creating one universal card with many flags.

My implementation order would be:

1. **Finish the frame contract.**  
   Make `WorkspaceScaffold` support primary destinations instead of assuming Profile. Establish one owner for headers, footers, and insets. Details and wizards get the appropriate frame without duplicating workspace chrome. Implement navigation presentation against fixture destinations, registering production destinations as screens become available.

2. **Complete ordinary form and selection controls.**  
   Extend existing fields and wizard components; add rows, chip groups, count steppers, and inline notices. Use Material switches, checkboxes, radios, sliders, tabs, and buttons directly wherever the theme already supplies everything needed.

3. **Implement shared formatting and picker behavior.**  
   Agree on CAD, dates, deadlines, timezone display, picker confirmation/dismissal, and field-error behavior. Keep date conversion and formatting separate from layout so they can be tested deterministically.

4. **Prepare reusable content compositions.**  
   Extend image presentation, then build the campsite/lottery/reservation components with realistic fixtures and explicit callbacks. Keep eligibility, filtering, saving, and lifecycle decisions in their owning controllers/domain code.

5. **Expand the gallery and review representative compositions.**  
   Include a short onboarding step, a longer form, a primary page with bottom navigation, a short lottery state, a summary/detail page, and a destructive dialog. This exposes inconsistencies before we repeat them across screens.

Maps should get their shared display/location-selection component before campsite location screens. QR, reviews, messaging composers, and export controls can follow their feature milestones; they do not need to delay the first screen batch.

For testing, I would establish the contracts before implementation:

| Area | Meaningful checks |
|---|---|
| Steppers and selection | Bounds, disabled actions, callbacks, supplied selection, accessible semantics |
| Forms and wizard actions | Editing, multiline content, IME behavior, custom action labels, submission blocking, dismissal |
| Pickers and formatting | Confirm versus Cancel, missing values, locale/timezone behavior, exact cents, date boundaries |
| Frames and navigation | Selected destination, Back callbacks, appropriate chrome, preserved controls during loading/failure |
| Summaries and images | Missing data/images, long values, optional actions, error recovery |
| Layout | Narrow phones, enlarged fonts/control sizes, keyboard visibility, reachable footer actions |

The existing [UI conventions](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/README.md) and gallery should become the component reference as this lands.

**This shared-controls work can be completed independently of teammates.** For example, a chip group receives selected IDs and emits a clicked ID; the reserved preference-toggle function remains a separate task. That preserves the agreed delegation model while making the surrounding UI ready.

Files: [WorkspaceScaffold.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/app/WorkspaceScaffold.kt) · [CampsiteCard.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/campsites/components/CampsiteCard.kt) · [AppScreen.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/components/layout/AppScreen.kt) · [StepScaffold.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/components/layout/StepScaffold.kt) · [StaySummary.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/components/summaries/StaySummary.kt) · [LotteryCard.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/lotteries/components/LotteryCard.kt) · [ReservationCard.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/reservations/components/ReservationCard.kt)

How would you organize and later invoke these components to ensure clean code organizaton ?

## Codex — 2026-10-24T17:59:25.046Z

I would keep **one app module, feature folders for screens, and a small shared component library organized by purpose**. The important rule is that each component has one owner and one clear public API.

The proposed layout would be:

```text
ui/
├── theme/                       Colors, typography, spacing, shapes
├── app/                         App root and workspace shell
├── components/
│   ├── layout/                  AppScreen, StepScaffold
│   ├── actions/                 Submitting buttons and action arrangements
│   ├── forms/                   Text fields, picker fields, count stepper
│   ├── selection/               Choice rows, preference chip groups
│   ├── rows/                    Navigation rows, settings rows
│   ├── summaries/               Detail rows, reusable stay summary
│   ├── feedback/                Notices, loading/empty/error, confirmations
│   ├── calendar/                Existing calendar
│   └── images/                  Existing image presentations
├── common/
│   ├── input/                   Existing pure input parsers
│   ├── format/                  CAD, date and time formatting
│   └── images/                  Existing image controller/state
├── onboarding/
│   ├── OnboardingRoute.kt
│   ├── OnboardingViewModel.kt
│   ├── OnboardingUiState.kt
│   └── steps/
├── campsites/
│   ├── CampsiteListRoute.kt
│   ├── CampsiteListScreen.kt
│   ├── CampsiteListViewModel.kt
│   └── components/              CampsiteCard, campsite-specific facts
├── lotteries/
│   └── components/              LotteryCard, lottery-specific presentation
└── reservations/
    └── components/              ReservationCard, payment-specific content
```

These are responsibility groups, not instructions to generate empty files. Existing shared files can move into these groups during the shared-controls pass, with their imports updated together.

**Feature components stay with their feature.** `CampsiteCard` belongs under campsites even when discovery and admin screens reuse it. Generic pieces such as its image presentation, status label, and summary rows belong in the shared library.

The invocation direction should always be:

```text
Navigation graph
    ↓
Route                  Connects ViewModel, navigation and Android launchers
    ↓
Screen / wizard step   Arranges supplied state and callbacks
    ↓
Feature components     CampsiteCard, LotteryCard, ReservationCard
    ↓
Shared components      Images, rows, fields, notices, buttons
    ↓
Material 3
```

For example, a proposed campsite list would look like this:

```kotlin
/** Connects observed campsite state to presentation.
 * @param viewModel Controller supplied by the route's factory.
 * @param onOpenCampsite Navigation callback receiving a stable identity.
 */
@Composable
fun CampsiteListRoute(
    viewModel: CampsiteListViewModel,
    onOpenCampsite: (String) -> Unit,
) {
    val state by viewModel.state.collectAsStateWithLifecycle()

    CampsiteListScreen(
        state = state,
        onRetry = viewModel::retry,
        onOpenCampsite = onOpenCampsite,
    )
}
```

Inside the screen, invocation stays straightforward:

```kotlin
items(state.campsites, key = { it.id }) { campsite ->
    CampsiteCard(
        campsite = campsite,
        onClick = { onOpenCampsite(campsite.id) },
    )
}
```

The card receives content and an action. It does not look up its campsite, create a repository, acquire a ViewModel, or navigate independently.

I would enforce these conventions:

| Concern | Owner |
|---|---|
| Saved data and coordinated operations | Repository |
| Screen draft, loading, submission and errors | Feature ViewModel |
| Navigation and platform launchers | Route |
| Screen composition | Screen or wizard step |
| Shared appearance and interaction behavior | Shared component |
| Temporary expanded menu or picker visibility | Component, when no other owner needs it |
| Formatting | Shared presentation helpers |
| Colors, typography, spacing and shapes | Theme |

For clean component APIs:

- **Supply values and callbacks.** A count stepper receives its value, bounds, enabled state, and change callback.
- **Keep selection controlled by the caller.** A chip group receives selected IDs and reports the clicked ID. It does not implement the reserved preference-toggle function.
- **Use slots for varying content.** A summary section can accept rows; a wizard footer can accept a secondary action. Avoid a growing collection of unrelated flags.
- **Keep navigation outside components.** Callbacks pass IDs upward rather than passing `NavController` downward.
- **Use Material directly where sufficient.** A plain `Switch` or `OutlinedButton` already gets the shared theme. An app wrapper should add useful layout, behavior, or semantics.
- **Keep helpers private and component APIs small.** Screens should depend on the intended entry composables, rather than assembling their internal pieces.

Frame ownership also needs to be explicit: **primary workspace pages use the workspace frame; details and wizards use their appropriate frame.** Content inside either frame does not add another app bar or repeat system insets.

Finally, the gallery should invoke the **same production components** used by screens, with fixture state and local demonstration callbacks. That gives us one place to review appearance and interaction, while component tests protect the shared behavior. A visual fix then propagates to every consumer through the existing API.

Files: [CampsiteCard.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/campsites/components/CampsiteCard.kt)

## Codex — 2026-10-24T18:30:17.994Z

I’d keep this within the existing `:app` module, with this structure:

```text
ui/
├── theme/                   Colors, typography, spacing, scaling
├── components/              Shared presentation controls
│   ├── layout/              AppScreen, StepScaffold
│   ├── actions/             AppActionButton
│   ├── forms/               Fields, count stepper
│   ├── selection/           Choices, switches, chips, dropdowns
│   ├── pickers/             Date, range, time
│   ├── feedback/            Loading, errors, notices, confirmation
│   ├── rows/
│   ├── summaries/
│   ├── images/
│   └── calendar/
├── common/                  Shared presentation logic, formatting
├── app/                     Root navigation and workspace shell
└── campsites/               Each feature owns its screens and state
    ├── CampsiteSetupRoute.kt
    ├── CampsiteSetupScreen.kt
    ├── CampsiteSetupViewModel.kt
    ├── CampsiteSetupUiState.kt
    └── components/          Campsite-specific reusable presentation
```

The feature screen files above illustrate the convention; they are future work.

Components are invoked through ordinary composable calls:

```kotlin
CountStepper(
    label = stringResource(R.string.guest_count),
    value = state.guestCount,
    minimum = 1,
    maximum = state.maximumGuests,
    onValueChange = onGuestCountChange,
)
```

The ownership chain is:

```text
Route → Screen → Feature components → Shared components → Material 3
```

- **Route:** obtains the ViewModel, collects lifecycle-aware state, connects navigation and Android launchers.
- **Screen:** arranges supplied state and callbacks. It can be previewed without Firebase.
- **Feature component:** understands campsite, lottery or reservation presentation.
- **Shared component:** renders values and reports user intent. It never saves data or navigates.

Form values belong to the ViewModel; temporary presentation state, such as an open dropdown, can stay inside the component. Dialogs keep draft selections until confirmation.

Use shared controls where they establish a useful convention, and Material directly for ordinary cases. Add a small parameter or content slot for a real variation; keep feature-specific variations beside their feature. This avoids both duplicated styling and a universal component with dozens of flags.

The current implementation follows this layout, and the debug gallery demonstrates calls to the actual production components.

## Codex — 2026-10-24T18:56:29.638Z

Keep the existing `:app` module, with these boundaries:

```text
ui/
├── theme/              Colors, typography, spacing, scaling
├── components/         Shared controls, grouped by purpose
├── common/             Formatting and presentation helpers
├── app/                Navigation and workspace shell
└── <feature>/
    ├── FeatureRoute.kt
    ├── FeatureScreen.kt
    ├── FeatureViewModel.kt
    ├── FeatureUiState.kt
    └── components/     Feature-specific reusable content
```

Invoke components through ordinary Compose calls:

```kotlin
CountStepper(
    label = stringResource(R.string.guests),
    value = state.guestCount,
    minimum = 1,
    maximum = state.maximumGuests,
    onValueChange = onGuestCountChange,
)
```

The ownership chain is:

```text
Route → Screen → Feature components → Shared controls → Material 3
```

- **Route:** connects ViewModel, lifecycle, navigation and platform launchers.
- **Screen:** arranges supplied state and callbacks.
- **ViewModel:** owns form values, validation and application actions.
- **Components:** render values and report intent. Temporary visual state can remain local.

Use shared controls for meaningful conventions; use Material directly otherwise. Keep feature-specific details alongside their feature, and apply the screen frame/insets once.

This organization and the two debug galleries are implemented and documented in [UI conventions](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/README.md).

Committed as `f0d94d0` on `part3/justin`. Visual inspection and independent review completed. All 139 unit, 43 device, 75 Firebase integration and 29 rules tests passed, along with lint, build and documentation checks.
