## Codex — 2026-10-24T19:11:51.547Z

**The wizard progress indicator is already included** in [StepScaffold.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/components/layout/StepScaffold.kt). My previous list wasn’t exhaustive.

Every wizard uses the same scaffold:

```kotlin
StepScaffold(
    title = title,
    progress = stepLabel,
    steps = StepProgress(current = 2, total = 4),
    onBack = onBack,
    onNext = onNext,
) {
    // This step’s fields.
}
```

It supplies:

- Segmented progress and accessible progress information.
- Step label, Back and Next.
- Optional Skip and Cancel.
- A final action label such as Publish.
- Submission blocking and scrolling when necessary.

The wizard owns its current step and branch-specific total. The indicator only displays them. Its rendering helper is private because screens reuse the complete scaffold.

You can inspect it in **Controls → Actions and progress → Preview a setup step**.

The library isn’t every component the finished app will need. It currently covers ordinary controls, wizard framing, feedback, calendars, images, navigation and several feature cards. Other cases fall into two categories:

- **Use Material directly:** ordinary tabs, badges, menus, dividers, snackbars and bottom sheets.
- **Build alongside their first feature:** featured carousel, map/location selection, QR integration, messaging and rating compositions.

The principle is to share recurring **interaction patterns and presentation**, including wizard progress, rather than only individual buttons and fields.

## Codex — 2026-10-24T19:14:05.604Z

**Next, use the shared controls to build the first complete feature: onboarding.** This will establish the pattern that subsequent screens follow.

The current bootstrap works, but `AppRoot` still displays `SetupRequiredScreen` when onboarding is needed. That is the clearest next integration point.

I would proceed in this order:

1. **Define the onboarding behavior and tests.** Cover entrant/owner branching, Back preserving input, optional interests, failed saves retaining input, interrupted setup resuming, and completion removing onboarding from Back history.

2. **Build the prototype-matching screen content.** Welcome, starting intent, name, interests and owner introduction. Reuse `StepScaffold`, choices, fields, chips and actions. Give each step realistic previews and Compose interaction tests.

3. **Implement one onboarding state owner.** One `OnboardingViewModel` owns the draft and progression across steps. Individual steps receive state and callbacks; they do not create their own ViewModels.

4. **Connect persistence and navigation.** Reuse the existing identity, account repository and setup-progress infrastructure. Test successful writes, retries and restoration. Register destinations as their screens become available.

5. **Review the running flow visually.** Compare it with the prototypes at normal and enlarged text sizes. Any recurring layout improvement belongs in the shared controls.

The resulting feature would look like:

```text
ui/onboarding/
├── OnboardingRoute.kt
├── OnboardingScreen.kt
├── OnboardingViewModel.kt
├── OnboardingUiState.kt
├── OnboardingDraft.kt
└── steps/
    ├── WelcomeStep.kt
    ├── IntentStep.kt
    ├── NameStep.kt
    ├── InterestsStep.kt
    └── OwnerIntroductionStep.kt
```

After onboarding, complete profile editing, then **owner campsite setup → entrant discovery/detail**, followed by the lottery and booking journey.

For teammate work, preserve the agreed small helper seams and prepare their contract tests alongside their first real consumer. The surrounding UI and wiring remain your responsibility.

**The immediate next deliverable should be the onboarding behavior/test checklist, before implementing its screens.**

## User — 2026-10-24T20:20:21.242Z

So, our plan is to build one complete onboarding screen, then afterwards when we established an example we can workon bulding other screens with tests and leave small homework for the teammates?

## Codex — 2026-10-24T20:20:47.017Z

Yes. **Build one complete onboarding flow as the reference implementation**, then use that pattern for the remaining features.

Onboarding includes several short screens: Welcome → starting intent → name → interests or owner introduction. Together, they demonstrate shared controls, navigation, ViewModel state, persistence, error handling and restoration.

Tests belong in that first implementation:

- **ViewModel tests:** branching, validation, retries and interrupted setup.
- **Compose tests:** inputs, actions, progress and navigation callbacks.
- **Firebase integration tests:** actual persistence behavior where needed.
- **Visual review:** prototype alignment and enlarged text.

Once that example is established, repeat this workflow for each feature:

```text
Define behavior and tests
        ↓
Build screen content and application wiring
        ↓
Isolate small, useful functions for teammates
        ↓
Provide failing contract tests and precise instructions
        ↓
Review their implementation and integrate
        ↓
Verify the complete feature
```

You own the architecture, screens, controllers, backend integration and broader tests. Teammates implement bounded functions with deterministic inputs and outputs, explicit edge cases and full contract coverage.

Two boundaries matter: **preserve already-working code**, and **leave existing reserved teammate functions untouched**. New assignments come from useful unfinished behavior.

That gives the team a working example to follow and keeps feature development moving while their small tasks proceed.

## User — 2026-10-24T20:24:38.562Z

For this module, how would you write the tests in advance? And generally, for uis, we probably should write ui tests afterwards?

## Codex — 2026-10-24T20:24:49.260Z

I’ll check the existing session and account contracts so the proposed onboarding tests cover observable behavior without tying us to a particular layout.

## Codex — 2026-10-24T20:25:39.137Z

**Define UI test scenarios upfront, then write executable Compose tests alongside each screen once its basic content exists.** We can write the logic tests before implementation because their contracts are easier to settle independently of layout.

For onboarding, I would split testing into four layers.

1. **Small function contracts, written first**

These are suitable for teammate assignments. For example, the reserved display-name function already has a clear contract:

| Input | Expected result |
| --- | --- |
| `""` or whitespace | Required |
| `"  Alex  "` | Valid name `"Alex"` |
| `"Alex  Chen"` | Preserve internal spaces |
| Unicode name | Accept without inventing restrictions |

Write executable tests, demonstrate that they fail for the unfinished behavior, then provide the implementation instructions. These tests should fully cover the small function’s agreed behavior.

2. **Onboarding ViewModel tests, written before its behavior**

Use the existing fake account repository, fake local preferences and coroutine test support. Agree on state and action APIs first, then implement in small test-first steps.

| Scenario | Observable expectation |
| --- | --- |
| Choose entrant | Name submission leads to interests |
| Choose owner | Name submission leads to owner setup |
| Go Back | Previously entered name and choices remain |
| Account creation is pending | Show submitting state; repeated Continue starts no additional operation |
| Account creation fails | Remain on name entry, retain input and offer retry |
| Profile observation emits during setup | Stay in onboarding until setup finishes |
| Owner activation fails | Preserve progress; do not enter an owner workspace |
| Skip optional interests | Finish without requiring preferences |
| Required completion write fails | Remain in setup and allow retry |
| Restore interrupted setup | Continue using the existing identity/profile |

These test outcomes rather than private methods or the exact internal state-management implementation. We should define precisely which draft values survive recreation before writing restoration assertions.

3. **Compose interaction tests, alongside the screen content**

Your instinct is right: writing detailed Compose tests before any screen exists often creates unnecessary churn. But I would finish them **before moving on to the next feature**, rather than postpone UI testing until all screens are built.

For each step:

```text
Agree behavior and scenarios
        ↓
Build minimal content with state and callbacks
        ↓
Write interaction tests
        ↓
Complete styling and application wiring
```

Examples:

- Selecting “Manage campsites” reports the correct choice.
- Editing the name reports the entered text.
- A supplied validation error is visible.
- Submitting state disables Continue.
- Skip appears only on the optional step.
- Enlarged text still allows reaching the primary action.

A screen-content test verifies callbacks and rendered state. A separate flow test uses the real ViewModel with fakes to verify that pressing Continue actually advances onboarding. This distinction avoids making a fake test host reproduce—and accidentally “prove”—our application logic.

4. **Integration and visual checks**

Extend the existing navigation tests to cover onboarding completion and Back history. Reuse existing Firebase account tests, adding integration cases only where the new workflow introduces an unverified persistence contract.

Inspect prototype alignment, spacing, keyboard behavior and enlarged text visually. Compose assertions should primarily use labels, semantics and outcomes, rather than exact positions or the number of layout containers.

**Our practical sequence is therefore: logic tests first; screen content and UI tests together; complete-flow tests during wiring; visual review before completion.**

Yes, the race is real at [AppViewModel.kt:138](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/app/AppViewModel.kt). The local completion flow can arrive before this ViewModel’s account listener sees OWNER. It then caches ENTRANT as the fallback, and keeps it after OWNER arrives. A previously cached workspace can also override a newer local preference after setup resumes.

Deriving the workspace from the latest observed roles and `local.workspace` fixes both cases. Existing write-gate and failure semantics remain intact: failed or pending DataStore writes leave the observed preference unchanged, so the prior selection remains active. The UI may briefly retain the old selection until the successful preference update reaches the collector.

Add deterministic tests for local completion arriving before the OWNER account update, and for a newer local choice overriding a cached selection after setup resumes. No files changed.

## Codex — 2026-10-24T21:13:41.590Z

“Display-name input” means the **Name field during onboarding**, such as “Alex Chen”. The reserved teammate function simply:

- Trims surrounding whitespace.
- Rejects an empty name.
- Otherwise returns the accepted name, preserving internal spaces and case.

It has nothing to do with login or authentication.

To try the onboarding on a running Android emulator or connected phone, run from the repository root:

```bash
nix develop .#ui
bash ./gradlew :app:installDebug

adb shell am start -S \
  -n io.github.CMPUT301F26stderr.campsbridge/.demo.OnboardingPreviewActivity \
  --ez interactive true
```

This launches an **interactive development flow without Firebase**. You can navigate Welcome → starting intent → Name, type, and go Back. **Continue at Name remains disabled** because you chose to leave that validation function for a teammate.

To visually inspect a later screen directly:

```bash
adb shell am start -S \
  -n io.github.CMPUT301F26stderr.campsbridge/.demo.OnboardingPreviewActivity \
  --es step INTERESTS
```

Replace `INTERESTS` with `WELCOME`, `INTENT`, `NAME`, or `OWNER`. These direct screen previews have passive callbacks, so their buttons do not advance or save. Add `--ez large true` to inspect enlarged text and controls.

The complete flow currently runs through automated tests using an injected name-validation fixture; manually completing the real flow will require integrating the reserved function.

## User — 2026-10-24T21:18:25.334Z

Why put it under debug?

## Codex — 2026-10-24T21:18:48.298Z

I put them there so the **preview fixtures and device demo screens stay out of release builds**. Android Studio can render previews from the debug source set, and our debug activity reuses the same content.

The downside is discoverability, as you just encountered.

For ordinary screen previews, placing `@Preview` functions beside their screen composables is simpler: you edit the screen and see its previews in the same file. Those previews should use small sample values without repositories or Firebase.

For this project, I’d refine the organization:

- **Beside each screen:** its `@Preview` functions and simple sample state.
- **Under `src/debug`:** interactive demo activities, fake repositories, and component galleries.
- **Under test source sets:** automated tests.

The current arrangement works, but keeping screen previews beside the screens would be easier for the team.

## User — 2026-10-24T21:19:14.758Z

What hapepns if these found their way into release?

## Codex — 2026-10-24T21:19:33.954Z

For ordinary `@Preview` functions, **usually nothing noticeable**:

- They don’t run automatically.
- They don’t create an accessible screen or navigation destination.
- Their code and sample values may add a small amount to the APK. Release shrinking can remove unused code.

Our release currently has optimization disabled, so unused preview code may remain packaged. For a student project, that small overhead is insignificant.

**Demo activities and fake repositories deserve separate treatment.** Activities registered in the release manifest could be opened, and fake behavior could accidentally become available. Those should remain under `src/debug`.

So keeping ordinary screen previews under `debug` provides little practical benefit here. Putting them beside their screens is reasonable and easier to work with.

## User — 2026-10-24T21:25:27.785Z

We can't ask teammates to input adb shell commands to bypass firebase. Is there a popular way to solve this, so for example debug builds jsut disables firebase or use a local simulation or whatever but it works with part of the functionality? And if not maybe we just keep empty the db when dev ends?

## Codex — 2026-10-24T21:25:31.115Z

I’ll check how the app selects Firebase and review the usual Android development setup, so I can suggest an approach teammates can launch with Android Studio’s Run button.


## Codex — 2026-10-24T21:26:45.136Z

Yes. A standard approach is a **demo build that uses local sample data**, alongside a build connected to Firebase. Google’s [Now in Android](https://github.com/android/nowinandroid#build) reference app uses this approach: `demo` supplies local data, and `prod` connects to its backend.

For our project, I’d set up:

| Variant | Data source | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| `demoDebug` | Local fixtures and in-memory repositories | Everyday UI development |
| `firebaseDebug` | Firebase | Test actual persistence and interactions between users |
| `firebaseRelease` | Firebase | Deliverable APK |

Teammates select **`demoDebug` once in Android Studio**, then press Run normally. Android supports selecting these configurations through [build variants](https://developer.android.com/build/build-variants).

The demo would use **the same screens and ViewModels**. Dependency wiring would supply different repository implementations:

```text
Screen → ViewModel → repository interface
                         ├── Demo implementation
                         └── Firebase implementation
```

For the demo, I would provide:

- Predictable sample data, initially covering onboarding and profile.
- Local interactions that update in-memory state.
- A clearly marked development menu to select scenarios: new account, existing entrant, existing owner.
- A reset button.
- Access to the component gallery and individual screen states.

Unimplemented functionality would remain explicitly pending. For example, the reserved name validator would still be pending; the screen chooser would let teammates inspect later onboarding screens directly.

Our ViewModels already accept repository interfaces. The main structural change would be removing `AppServices`’ dependency on the concrete Firebase `AppContainer`, allowing dependency wiring to supply either implementation. We would add demo behavior incrementally as features become available.

**Keeping Firebase empty would not solve the same problem.** The app would still need Firebase connectivity, and most screens would show empty states. Using shared Firebase for integration testing is perfectly reasonable for this project, but resetting and reseeding should be deliberate, rather than something performed automatically when someone finishes development.

My recommendation is **a runnable offline demo for routine UI work, plus Firebase for persistence testing**. No ADB commands required for teammates.

Files: [AppContainer.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/di/AppContainer.kt) · [AppServices.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/di/AppServices.kt)

Currently, onboarding provides the first working example, while Profile mainly displays information and manages photos. Explore and campsite setup still need their feature screens and wiring.

Work in this order:

1. **Profile → personal details → contact details.** Match the prototypes, introduce editable drafts, and save through the existing account repository. Cover loading, validation feedback, saving, failure, and retry.
2. **Settings and policies.** Add their navigation and approved content. Connect settings to the existing preferences model.
3. **Owner campsite setup.** Build the first substantial feature: campsite list, setup steps, review, and publication. This will exercise the shared calendar, images, numeric inputs, and wizard controls.

For the immediate Profile slice, establish these tests before implementation:

- Existing account values populate the form.
- Editing remains local until Save.
- Successful saving updates the displayed profile.
- Failure preserves input and permits retry.
- Repeated taps cannot submit duplicate operations.
- Compose tests verify editing, navigation, and feedback.

Keep the reserved display-name function pending for your teammate. Its unfinished implementation should not prevent you from building the surrounding screens and testing their behavior through an injected implementation.

**There is no need for another broad scaffolding phase.** Add missing shared pieces when these concrete screens reveal a repeated need. The next manageable change is personal and contact editing, with previews, controller tests, and Compose interaction tests.

## Codex — 2026-10-24T22:12:30.501Z

For the **November 6 Halfway checkpoint**, the agreed scope is below, based on the [Wiki release plan](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/wiki/Product-Backlog#part-3-halfway-checkpoint-november-6) and [implementation plan](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/doc/part3_implementation_plan.md). This lists planned delivery, including work still pending.

**1. Startup and application shell**

- Startup/loading.
- Initialization failure with Retry.
- Removed/unavailable account.
- Entrant, owner, and admin workspace shells.
- Workspace/role selection.

**2. Onboarding**

- Welcome.
- Choose starting intent: entrant or owner.
- Enter name.
- Optional camping interests.
- Owner introduction: create first campsite or set up later.
- Guidelines reader accessible during onboarding.

**3. Profile and policies**

- Profile menu.
- Personal details: name and profile photo.
- Contact details: optional email and phone.
- Settings menu containing implemented destinations.
- Notification settings: alert preference and Android permission status.
- Policies menu.
- Policy/guidelines reader.

Accessibility-setting pages and account deletion remain outside the Halfway commitment. The shared theme already supports scaling and color-mode inputs.

**4. Owner campsite management**

- My campsites: drafts and published listings.
- Manage campsite overview.
- Campsite setup, with separate steps:
  - Basics: name and description.
  - Photo.
  - Location and timezone.
  - Capacity and nightly price.
  - Amenities and tags.
  - Rules.
  - Offered-night availability.
  - Review, save draft, and publish.
- Availability editor for an existing campsite.
- Listing preview using the shared campsite detail screens.

General editing of published campsite details remains Final scope; offered-night editing is included now.

**5. Discovery and campsite inspection**

- Explore/search results.
- Search filters: location, dates, and guests.
- Campsite overview.
- About campsite.
- Campsite map.
- Read-only availability calendar.
- Campsite rules and refund policy.
- Lottery selection when a campsite has multiple relevant lotteries.

Featured discovery, ratings/review interactions, QR scanning, and additional WOW discovery features are not Halfway commitments.

**6. Owner lottery management**

- My lotteries.
- Single-campsite lottery setup:
  - Basics.
  - Select campsite and allocate offered nights.
  - Registration window.
  - Winner booking window.
  - Maximum winners.
  - Review, save draft, and publish.
- Manage lottery: configuration summary, lifecycle, and draw status.
- Participants and results.
- Manual draw confirmation/progress where applicable.

Multiple-campsite setup and configurable waitlist limits remain Final scope.

**7. Entrant lottery participation**

- My lotteries.
- Lottery overview with join/leave actions.
- Lottery schedule and details.
- Relevant campsite/offered-night information.

The overview needs preview states for upcoming registration, open registration, joined, registration closed, drawing, won before booking opens, ready to book, lost, expired, and already booked. These are variants of the same screen.

**8. Booking**

- Choose stay dates.
- Enter guest count.
- Read and acknowledge rules/policy.
- Review price and confirm simulated payment.
- Confirming, conflict, changed-price, expired-opportunity, and uncertain-result states.

Halfway uses one campsite per lottery, so a separate campsite-selection step is unnecessary.

**9. Reservations**

- Entrant active reservations.
- Owner active reservations, including campsite filtering.
- Shared reservation overview with entrant/owner actions.
- Payment and refund details.
- Historically accepted rules and policy.
- Entrant cancellation confirmation.
- Owner rejection confirmation.
- Updated cancelled/refunded reservation detail.

Dedicated Past/Cancelled browsing tabs, reservation maps, CSV export, and messaging remain Final scope.

**10. Inbox and alerts**

- Persistent notification Inbox.
- Explicit winning-notification and losing-notification examples.
- Unread/read presentation.
- Loading, empty, retrieval failure, and unavailable destination.
- Navigation from a notification to its lottery or reservation.
- Android alert presentation and notification entry behavior.

Owner announcements, sent history, and conversations remain Final scope. Closed-app push delivery is not required for Halfway.

**11. Read-only administration**

- Campsite browsing and shared campsite inspection.
- Profile browsing, search/role filtering, and profile inspection.
- Uploaded-image browsing and image inspection.
- Notification log.
- Notification event detail, including available audience information.

Removal actions and message logs remain Final scope.

For the **shared previewable parts**, we agreed to deliver:

| Family | Parts |
| --- | --- |
| Theme | Colors, Inter typography, shapes, spacing, enlarged text/control presentation |
| Layout/navigation | Screen frame, workspace navigation, menu/detail rows, wizard scaffold and step progress |
| Actions/forms | Button hierarchy, submitting actions, text fields, errors, count stepper, price entry |
| Selection | Choice rows, switches, checkboxes, preference chips, dropdowns |
| Pickers/calendar | Date/range/time dialogs, availability calendar, month navigation, legends and selection states |
| Images | Avatar, local/remote photos, missing/loading/error states, selection/save/remove presentation |
| Feature content | Campsite, lottery and reservation cards; discovery toolbar; stay and price summaries; notification rows |
| Feedback | Loading, empty, retry, inline notices, status labels, confirmations and submission progress |
| Development hosts | Component gallery, onboarding previews, and offline scenarios for implemented flows |

Each applicable screen should have realistic content, loading, empty, failure, and submission previews, plus long-text/enlarged-font checks. System Photo Picker, live Maps, Android permissions, and OS notifications also need running-app verification.

The checkpoint also requires the **working Firebase-connected flow behind these screens**: identity restoration, persistence, foreground draws, notification delivery, atomic booking/payment, cancellation/refunds, navigation, and automated tests. Preview coverage alone does not complete that delivery.

## Codex — 2026-10-24T22:26:44.992Z

That sounds coherent. You’re prioritizing features that make discovery and communication feel complete.

| Decision | Features |
|---|---|
| **Keep as intended product features** | Two-way reservation messaging; review threads and reactions; campsite maps; reservation maps; nearby campsite discovery |
| **Drop from planned UI work** | Adjustable font/control settings; color-blind settings; campsite match scores |
| **Remain optional** | Featured carousel |

Messaging, threads, and reactions already have substantial persistence implementations. Most remaining work is their screens, controllers, navigation, and interaction tests.

I agree that nearby discovery becomes a relatively small extension once maps are working. The additional pieces are current-location retrieval, permission handling, radius selection, distance filtering, and a fallback when location is unavailable. For our small dataset, we can initially filter loaded campsites by distance without introducing a spatial-query system.

I’d organize the work around three shared foundations:

- **Maps:** one integration reused for campsite inspection, location selection, nearby discovery, and reservation maps.
- **Reviews:** build the required rating/review flow, then add reactions and threaded replies through the existing repository.
- **Messaging:** build one reservation conversation supporting both participants from the outset.

The existing theme/scaling support can stay; we simply stop spending effort on its optional settings pages.

These become our **intended final product scope**. I wouldn’t automatically move all of them into the Halfway commitment: first complete the agreed campsite → lottery → booking flow, then integrate these extensions as their dependencies become ready. Their WOW labels remain useful for demonstrating requirement coverage, even though you consider them essential to the product.

## Codex — 2026-10-24T22:52:21.792Z

I’d build **complete feature slices**, leaving small, isolated functions for teammates. You can finish the screens, controllers, navigation, persistence wiring, and surrounding tests without waiting for those functions.

A feature with a missing helper remains explicitly pending until that helper is integrated. We should distinguish **your surrounding implementation being ready** from **the whole feature being complete**.

The existing foundation is sufficient: theme, controls, navigation structure, onboarding, images, calendars, parsing, repository implementations, test infrastructure, and offline development host. We extend these as features need them.

For every feature, follow this cycle:

1. **Define behavior:** inputs, actions, navigation, saved state, errors, and completion conditions.
2. **Write tests:** deterministic rules and ViewModel tests first; identify the Compose interactions to verify.
3. **Implement:** previewable content, controller, route, repository integration, and useful offline scenarios.
4. **Prepare teammate gaps:** freeze their contracts and write executable failing tests before handing them over.
5. **Verify:** relevant unit, Compose, Firebase, and Android integration checks.
6. **Call a bounded reviewer:** inspect the feature’s correctness, boundaries, and missing tests.
7. **Fix findings and commit:** stage only that feature’s changes, preserving work from other threads.

Use the existing package boundaries and a predictable feature layout:

```text
ui/<feature>/
    FeatureRoute.kt
    FeatureScreen.kt          # State + callbacks; previews at the bottom
    FeatureViewModel.kt
    FeatureUiState.kt
    FeatureDraft.kt           # Only for an editor or wizard
    components/               # Feature-specific reusable content
    logic/                    # Actual small rules and teammate functions
```

Static menus/readers do not need a ViewModel automatically. Each wizard gets one state owner. Firebase remains behind repositories; Android integrations remain behind platform adapters.

The first sequence completes the Halfway scope:

| Order | Feature slice | Implementation | Tests agreed before development |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Profile and policies** | Profile menu, personal/contact editors, settings entry, policy menu/readers. Extend the existing profile/photo flow. | Populate saved values; cancel preserves saved data; failed save retains input; duplicate submission blocked; unrelated account fields preserved; Back returns correctly. |
| **2** | **Owner campsite setup** | Campsite list, setup steps, review/publication, management overview, availability editor. Introduce the shared map/location-selection boundary here. | Draft retained between steps; stable identity across retries; unpublished drafts excluded from discovery; image handling; occupied nights protected; partial calendar saves remain recoverable. |
| **3** | **Discovery and campsite details** | Explore, search/filter editing, results, campsite overview, About, Map, Availability, Rules, and relevant lottery selection. | Filter boundaries; all requested stay nights checked; no-results versus failure; search context survives detail navigation; missing photo/listing handled. |
| **4** | **Lottery management and participation** | Owner list/setup/review, management, participants, entrant list/detail, join/leave, and draw presentation. Connect existing foreground processing. | Exact registration/booking boundaries; stable creation retries; repeated join/leave; failed actions; completed-draw gating; winner/loser states; foreground and manual draw agreement. |
| **5** | **Inbox and saved-record destinations** | Persistent notification Inbox, unread presentation, lottery/reservation destinations, reservation overview, payment details, and accepted terms. | Event/receipt assembly; missing context; first-read behavior; stable ordering; correct destination; historical terms/payment displayed from saved records. |
| **6** | **Booking and reservation management** | Dates → guests → terms → review/confirm; entrant/owner active lists; cancellation/rejection and refund presentation. | Consecutive eligible nights; checkout exclusion; changed quote/terms; concurrent booking conflict; uncertain confirmation; duplicate submission; full refund and inventory release. |
| **7** | **Alerts and read-only administration** | Notification preference, Android alerts and entry handling; campsite/profile/image browsing and notification logs. | Preference persistence; permission denial; no old-alert replay; cold/warm notification entry; admin inspection causes no writes; missing records/objects remain recoverable. |

Repository contracts already added during foundation work should be reused. Changes belong alongside their first actual consumer rather than in another general persistence rewrite.

After those foundations, continue into the selected Final scope. If a Halfway helper is awaiting a teammate, you can progress into these slices:

| Feature slice | Work |
|---|---|
| **Management completion** | Campsite/lottery editing, multiple-campsite allocation and booking, waitlist limits, and remaining management actions. Reuse wizard sections as focused editors. |
| **Reservation history and maps** | Past/Cancelled categories and reservation maps using booked location snapshots. Preserve category and individual stay selection. |
| **Nearby discovery** | Current-location adapter, permission/fallback handling, radius selection, distance filtering, and map previews. Reuse the existing map integration. |
| **Ratings, reactions, and threads** | Rating editor/list, reaction controls, reply composer, parent context, and thread presentation using existing repositories. |
| **Reservation messaging** | One conversation per reservation, both participants’ composers, Inbox entry points, first-read acknowledgement, and send/retry states. |
| **Announcements** | Compose, audience context, sent history, and recipient detail. Preserve the distinction between publication and receipt delivery. |
| **QR and CSV** | Stable campsite identity encoding/scanning; reservation export through Android save/share surfaces. Add intent tests and deterministic format tests. |
| **Removal and remaining admin work** | Self-removal, administrative removal, resumable cleanup presentation, and read-only message logs. Reuse existing coordinated operations. |

For teammate work, I would reserve the following **candidate gaps**. Aim for two or three useful functions per feature area, rather than forcing that number onto every screen.

| Area | Suitable small functions |
|---|---|
| **Profile** | Existing reserved display-name parser; optional-contact normalization. |
| **Campsites** | Calculate requested availability changes still unconfirmed; stable campsite-name ordering, shared with discovery. |
| **Discovery** | Text-match predicate; guest-capacity predicate. |
| **Lotteries** | Field-specific window-validation feedback; participant filtering by selected entry state. |
| **Inbox** | Unread receipt count; ordering assembled Inbox rows. |
| **Booking/reservations** | Available-night intersection; reservation category from supplied local date; check-in ordering. |
| **Administration** | Profile-role filtering; agreed profile-search predicate. |
| **Reviews** | Next reaction selection; reaction counts; grouping replies by parent ID. |
| **Messaging** | Composer readiness; selecting incoming unread message IDs for the controller to acknowledge. |
| **Announcements** | Title/body validation feedback; filtering sent announcements to a selected lottery. |
| **QR/export** | QR payload parsing after we settle its format; CSV field escaping. |

I would keep transaction coordination, quotes, timezone conversion, lifecycle scheduling, map/location integration, upload reconciliation, and removal workflows with you.

Two corrections to the older task pool: **workspace selection and onboarding tag toggling already work**. Reuse them; do not remove them or manufacture replacement assignments.

Each delegated function needs:

- One permitted source file and a fixed signature.
- Explicit valid, invalid, empty, and boundary behavior.
- A worked example and exact test command.
- Immutable inputs and deterministic outputs.
- A real production caller.
- **100% measured source-line and branch coverage**, added to the central coverage gate when its task is prepared.

The handoff should preserve green integration checks:

1. Commit the surrounding feature with its pending dependency explicit.
2. Prepare the compiling stub and failing contract tests separately. The teammate runs this initial failing stage locally.
3. Supply the tests/stub as a ready patch or equivalent handoff; their implementation PR must pass the mandatory checks.
4. Review the implementation, verify coverage, integrate it, and rerun the actual feature journey.

Previews and controller tests can supply known fixture responses while a helper is pending. Production must not contain a second implementation that quietly bypasses the assignment.

**The first implementation batch should be Profile menu → personal details → contact details → policies.** It extends working code, establishes the editing pattern for later features, and gives the two existing profile tasks concrete production consumers.

## User — 2026-10-24T22:59:01.219Z

For in between feature transitions, how would you keep trackof the ui state?

## Codex — 2026-10-24T23:00:23.159Z

I would keep state with **the screen or workflow that owns it**, and pass IDs or small results between features. Shared application facts come from repositories.

| State | Owner | Lifetime |
|---|---|---|
| Identity, account, workspace | Existing `AppViewModel` | Current application session |
| Search filters, loaded results, form/submission state | Feature ViewModel | While its navigation entry remains |
| Multi-step wizard draft | One ViewModel scoped to the wizard | Until completion or explicit abandonment |
| Scroll position, expanded menu, selected tab | Compose state, saveable where appropriate | Relevant screen/navigation state |
| Confirmed profiles, campsites, entries, reservations | Repository | Persisted independently of navigation |

**ViewModels keep live state; `SavedStateHandle` restores selected small values.** Save draft text, selected IDs, dates, and the current step there when restoration is needed. Reload complete records through repositories. Don’t put images, repositories, or entire database records into navigation arguments.

Here is how the important transitions would work:

**Explore → campsite → Back**

Explore retains its filters, search term, and list position. Navigation passes `campsiteId`. Campsite details has its own ViewModel and observes that record. Back reveals the existing Explore state.

Opening details should not recreate or reset the search workflow.

**Explore → filters → Apply/Cancel**

The filter editor owns an editable draft.

- Apply returns the selected values to Explore.
- Cancel leaves the applied filters unchanged.
- Opening filters again starts from the currently applied values.

If the editor belongs to the same navigation graph, its parent ViewModel can own both applied filters and the temporary draft.

**Campsite setup → location picker → setup**

One wizard ViewModel owns the campsite draft. The picker returns coordinates and the selected label to that owner. Moving between steps does not create separate copies of the draft.

We already use this general ownership pattern for onboarding.

**Lottery → booking → reservation**

Booking receives the lottery identity and loads the current opportunity and inventory. One booking ViewModel owns dates, guests, accepted terms, and submission state.

After confirmed success:

1. Navigate using the returned `reservationId`.
2. Remove the completed booking wizard from Back history.
3. Show the reservation through repository observation.

The lottery and reservation screens update from saved data; they do not exchange ViewModel state directly.

**Profile editing → Profile**

The editor keeps unsaved changes locally. Save calls the account repository. Profile observes the updated account. There is no need to manually copy the updated name into several screens.

For transitions returning a small selection, use an explicit result addressed to the actual caller. Consume it once. Avoid a global event bus or a shared ViewModel containing every feature’s state.

For workspace tabs, preserve each workspace’s navigation and screen state. Switching Owner → Entrant → Owner should restore the owner context. Keep that state scoped to the account so another account cannot inherit it.

Before implementing each feature, we should specify and test:

- Who owns its state.
- Which inputs survive Back and recreation.
- What navigation arguments/results it accepts.
- What completion or cancellation clears.
- How repository updates affect an open screen.

That gives us predictable transitions without introducing a large application-wide state machine.

Files: [AppViewModel.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/app/AppViewModel.kt)

I found three actionable gaps:

- **P1 — The proposed cancellation cutoff is bypassable through Firestore.** The plan puts the new local-checkout cutoff in the repository ([implementation plan](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/doc/part3_implementation_plan.md:230)), but Firestore rules currently authorize participant/admin cancellation without checking checkout time ([firestore.rules](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/firebase/firestore.rules)). A client can write directly and bypass the repository. Add a rule-enforceable cutoff or trusted server operation, plus a rules test that attempts cancellation at and after local checkout.
- **P2 — Admin audience reads can race delivery and report an incomplete audience.** The plan combines queued recipient sets and Inbox receipts ([implementation plan](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/doc/part3_implementation_plan.md:265)), but delivery writes receipts one by one and deletes the job only after fan-out completes ([NotificationDeliveries.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/data/notification/NotificationDeliveries.kt)). If `getAudience` reads receipts first, then the job after delivery finishes, it can miss recipients without detecting the gap. Specify server-only reads in job-then-receipts order, and add a partial-fan-out/completion case to [the audience tests](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/doc/part3_feature_test_plan.md:191). The existing server-only document and query helpers are in [FirestoreAccess.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/data/firestore/FirestoreAccess.kt).
- **P2 — Active-list tests don’t require excluding past and cancelled reservations.** The plan says past/cancelled browsing is Final ([implementation plan](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/doc/part3_implementation_plan.md:229)), while the existing repository observations include retained history. The list contract tests cover ordering and category but don’t explicitly assert that completed or cancelled stays are absent from the Halfway active lists ([feature test plan](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/doc/part3_feature_test_plan.md:158)). Add a list ViewModel case with upcoming, in-progress, past, and cancelled records.

Both plan files are ignored by `.gitignore` at [line 347](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/.gitignore:347), so they won’t appear in a PR unless that is addressed deliberately. The docs otherwise correctly distinguish proposed `getForOpportunity` from existing APIs, and they mark the Firebase Android journey’s command as pending. I made no changes and ran no tests.

**Coverage gap — keyboard reachability for the new footer action.** The existing keyboard test at [StepScaffoldTest.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/androidTest/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/components/StepScaffoldTest.kt) uses the old single-action configuration. The large-text review test keeps both actions visible with the keyboard closed. Neither verifies that Save draft remains reachable with the IME open after the extra footer action is added. This is an unverified behavior risk, not an observed failure.

The new API otherwise preserves defaults and existing trailing-lambda callers, uses the outlined secondary style, independently gates the actions, blocks both during submission, rejects Skip combinations, and documents the added parameters. The preview uses no-op callbacks and does not simulate persistence. The tests do not directly assert the incomplete label/callback and Skip/secondary rejection invariants, though production code enforces them.

## Agent message — 2026-10-25T01:39:08.830Z

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/campsite_input_review
Payload:
One finding: `CampsiteDetailsInput.kt:44-50` accepts fixed-offset zone IDs such as `+05:30`, because `ZoneId.of` accepts them. M2a requires an IANA timezone; accepting a fixed offset can misinterpret local campsite dates when regional daylight-saving rules apply. Reproduce with `validateCampsiteDetails(validDraft().copy(timeZone = "+05:30"))`, which returns `Value`.

I found no other correctness or KDoc issues in the three reviewed files. Coordinates are validated by their constructor, and the parser reuse, optional-field handling, and preservation of unlisted option IDs match the stated scope. No files were modified and no builds or tests were run.

Files: [CampsiteDetailsInput.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/campsites/logic/CampsiteDetailsInput.kt)

2. **The M2a draft and validation entries don’t match the code already present.** The plan says `stable id?` and describes a `validatedDetails()` helper ([lines 322, 368](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/doc/part3_implementation_plan.md:322)). `CampsiteDraft.id` already exists, and the lead-owned API is the top-level `validateCampsiteDetails(draft)` ([CampsiteDraft.kt:34](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/campsites/CampsiteDraft.kt), [CampsiteDetailsInput.kt:40](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/campsites/logic/CampsiteDetailsInput.kt)). Update the target shape to extend these types and use the existing validator; otherwise the plan invites duplicate draft or validation APIs.

3. **M2a dependency wiring needs a concrete factory shape.** The plan calls for `Clock` in the setup ViewModel and adding campsite repositories to factories/debug/test bindings ([lines 323, 332](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/doc/part3_implementation_plan.md:323)), but the current `AppServices` has no `Clock` ([AppServices.kt:12](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/di/AppServices.kt)), and `AppRepositories` exposes only accounts, images, and pending work ([AppRepositories.kt:8](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/di/AppRepositories.kt)). Name the setup/list/detail factory entry points and say which dependencies they receive; also specify which debug and Android test hosts need the new repository bindings. The broader DI section proposes a shared clock, but M2a doesn’t connect that proposal to these factories.

4. **Clarify the M2b detail’s staged lottery dependency.** The detail target includes active-lottery context ([plan line 417](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/doc/part3_implementation_plan.md:417)), while M2b’s ViewModel dependencies omit `LotteryRepository` ([line 415](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/doc/part3_implementation_plan.md:415)); later text correctly defers lottery choices and Join behavior until M3a ([lines 407, 429, 447](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/doc/part3_implementation_plan.md:407)). State explicitly that M2b’s detail omits lottery state until M3a, then extends the existing owner when that repository is wired.

These are documentation-level findings; I made no changes and ran no tests.

## Agent message — 2026-10-25T01:50:58.905Z

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER

Sender: /root/plan_shared_control_refs_oct24
Payload:
## Existing controls to reuse

There is no `ui/common/options` package. Shared selection controls are in `ui/components/selection`; pure count and price parsing is in `ui/common/input`.

| Area | Existing APIs and file | Boundary for upcoming screens |
|---|---|---|
| Screen and wizard shell | `AppScreen` (`ui/components/layout/AppScreen.kt:43`); `StepScaffold`, `StepProgress`, `StepAction` (`ui/components/layout/StepScaffold.kt:54, 139, 145`) | `StepScaffold` already supplies scrollable step content, a fixed action footer, progress segments, optional Skip/Cancel, and distinct primary/secondary review actions such as Publish and Save draft. Caller supplies values and callbacks; it does not save or navigate. |
| Buttons | `AppActionButton`, `ActionStyle` (`ui/components/actions/AppActions.kt:24, 37`) | Use for actions outside the wizard footer and to keep primary, secondary, text, destructive, and submitting appearances consistent. |
| Text and count inputs | `AppTextField` (`ui/components/forms/FormFields.kt:32`); `CountStepper` (`ui/components/forms/CountStepper.kt:31`) | Text input supports error/helper text, multiline input, units, and keyboard options. `CountStepper` is bounded and caller-controlled; its caller owns validation and state. |
| Selection and dropdowns | `ChoiceOption`, `ChoiceRow`, `SwitchRow`, `CheckboxRow`, `PreferenceChips` (`ui/components/selection/ChoiceControls.kt:25, 39, 70, 100, 128`); `DropdownField` (`ui/components/selection/DropdownField.kt:31`) | Use stable option IDs with labels supplied by the caller. Components report user choices and do not update drafts or persistence themselves. |
| Date and time input | `PickerField` (`ui/components/pickers/PickerFields.kt:29`); `DateInputDialog`, `DateRangeInputDialog`, `TimeInputDialog` (`ui/components/pickers/PickerDialogs.kt:46, 75, 140`); `pickerMillisToDate`, `dateToPickerMillis` (`ui/common/DateInputs.kt:11, 18`) | Dialog edits are temporary until Confirm; dismiss leaves the applied value alone. The range dialog returns arrival and exclusive checkout, but does not check availability. |
| Availability calendar | `CampsiteCalendar` (`ui/components/calendar/CampsiteCalendar.kt:62`); `CalendarState`, `CalendarDayState`, `CalendarDayStatus` (`ui/components/calendar/CalendarState.kt:8, 15, 28`); `CalendarSelection` (`ui/components/calendar/CalendarSelection.kt:6`); `buildMonthCells` (`ui/components/calendar/MonthCells.kt:12`) | The component renders caller-provided month, inventory, selection, and selectable dates. Callbacks request month changes and date selection; the caller owns selection rules, inventory meaning, and saving. Statuses currently cover unavailable, available, and occupied. |
| Summaries and cards | `DetailRow`, `StaySummary` (`ui/components/summaries/DetailRow.kt:22`, `StaySummary.kt:28`); `CampsiteCard`, `CampsiteRow` (`ui/campsites/components/CampsiteCard.kt:35, 88`); `LotteryCard` (`ui/lotteries/components/LotteryCard.kt:23`); `ReservationCard` (`ui/reservations/components/ReservationCard.kt:27`) | Use `CampsiteRow` for compact owner listings and the feature cards where their existing facts fit. Status and deadline text are supplied by the caller; cards do not infer lifecycle state. `StaySummary` formats a selected stay and total. |
| Status and feedback | `StatusLabel` (`ui/components/feedback/StatusLabel.kt:17`); `LoadingContent`, `EmptyContent`, `FailureContent` (`FeedbackContent.kt:18, 30, 41`); `InlineNotice`, `NoticeTone` (`InlineNotice.kt:15, 25`); `ConfirmationDialog` (`ConfirmationDialog.kt:24`) | Reuse for distinct loading/empty/failure states, inline recovery, icon-plus-text status, and confirmations. Keep the host screen and its inputs intact around them. |
| Images | `ImageSlot` (`ui/components/images/ImageSlot.kt:66`); `RemoteImage` (`ui/components/images/RemoteImage.kt:38`); `ImageSlotState` (`ui/common/images/ImageSlotState.kt:27`); `ImageSlotController` (`ui/common/images/ImageSlotController.kt:27`) | `ImageSlot` presents image selection and explicit save/remove/retry actions. A route/ViewModel supplies the system photo picker and callbacks. `RemoteImage` only renders a provided reference; it does not look up image records. The controller owns one immutable image target and should be closed with its parent lifetime. |
| Shared display rules | `formatCad`, `formatDate`, `formatDateTime`, `resolveDateTime` (`ui/common/format/DisplayFormats.kt:16, 33, 44, 66`); `Spacing`, `CampsbridgeTheme`, `appTypography` (`ui/theme/Spacing.kt:6`, `Theme.kt:21`, `Type.kt:22`) | Reuse shared money/date formatting, spacing, theme colors, typography, and shapes instead of matching colors or spacing from individual Stitch exports. |
| Pure form parsing | `PositiveCountInput`, `parsePositiveCount` (`ui/common/input/PositiveCountInput.kt:4, 20`); `PriceInput`, `parsePriceCents` (`ui/common/input/PriceInput.kt:8, 23`) | Keep parsing and validation in the ViewModel/controller layer; expose raw text and field errors to the screen. These functions already have JVM tests. |

Relevant existing tests include `StepScaffoldTest`, `PickerDialogsTest`, `CampsiteCalendarTest`, `CalendarSelectionTest`, `MonthCellsTest`, `PositiveCountInputTest`, `PriceInputTest`, and `ImageSlotControllerTest`. They cover wizard actions and reachability, picker apply/dismiss behavior, calendar rendering/selection, input parsing, and image operation outcomes.

## Stale or potentially misleading design notes

- [DESIGNS.md:85–87](doc/screens/DESIGNS.md) says the calendar implementation choice “remains pending a small working prototype.” A Compose `CampsiteCalendar` with month navigation, status legend, selection semantics, previews, and UI tests already exists. The actual **prototype/layout choice is no longer pending**; the calendar’s feature-specific inventory and persistence behavior still belongs to its callers.
- [DESIGNS.md:113](doc/screens/DESIGNS.md) asks for a “calendar prototype” as part of a future component gallery. Existing calendar previews and UI tests cover a basic visual/interaction prototype. A centralized gallery itself is not evident, so the plan should not imply all calendar work is complete or require a new gallery solely to repeat those previews.
- [doc/screens/stitch/CONSISTENCY.md:9,31](doc/screens/stitch/CONSISTENCY.md) repeats that a shared availability component must be prototyped and that calendar library/layout selection is runtime work. The calendar layout is already implemented with Compose layouts; selection-specific allocation, eligibility, and partial-save behavior remains feature work.
- [DESIGNS.md:50](doc/screens/DESIGNS.md) explicitly says its inventory describes responsibilities rather than existing APIs. That is accurate, but for implementation planning it should be supplemented with the concrete API inventory above so the team does not rebuild controls that already exist.

Recommended planning rule: first compose these controls into feature screens. Add or change a shared component only when the current API cannot express a requirement or when a repeated visual/interaction rule needs a real shared owner. Do not treat the generic calendar as implementing date eligibility or save/retry rules.

## Agent message — 2026-10-25T01:59:26.243Z

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/expanded_plan_review_oct24
Payload:
The graph ambiguity is fixed in the current plan: management and preview use separate context-qualified detail routes, and the availability editor is distinct from the read-only calendar section.

Four issues remain before the plan is an unambiguous implementation contract:

1. **M4b defines two incompatible `NotificationAudience` APIs.** [part3_implementation_plan.md:995](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/doc/part3_implementation_plan.md:995) specifies `Available(eventId, capturedAudienceKnown, members) | Unavailable`; line 1000 specifies `Recorded(members) | NoRecordedInformation | Unavailable`. That changes what the repository, UI and tests must implement. Choose one result type and align the “no recorded information” case and tests with it.

2. **M3b disagrees on whether marking read happens before or after navigation.** [part3_implementation_plan.md:674](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/doc/part3_implementation_plan.md:674) says acknowledge, then navigate; line 706 says navigate, then acknowledge. Both say acknowledgement failure must not block navigation, but the ordering and `openThenAcknowledge` test contract should be explicit and consistent.

3. **The factory instructions omit the actual debug composition site.** [part3_implementation_plan.md:170](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/doc/part3_implementation_plan.md:170) names `OfflineDemoActivity/DemoRepositories`, but `AppServices` and the private demo repository implementation are created in [DemoSession.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/debug/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/demo/DemoSession.kt) and line 121. Adding `Clock` or repository ports without updating `DemoSession.kt` will leave the debug host incomplete.

4. **Two fixture assertions do not follow from their stated inputs.** In [part3_feature_test_plan.md:65](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/doc/part3_feature_test_plan.md:65), F20 should count two unread receipts, but only N2 and N4 are specified as unread; set N1 and N3 to read. At [line 62](/home/admin/Projects/stderr-app/doc/part3_feature_test_plan.md:62), “first call succeeds, second fails” relies on a Map iteration order. Make outcomes date-specific or specify the write ordering.

The revised prototype normalization and the other inspected repository, calendar, and model references align with the current code. I made no edits and ran no builds or tests.

Files: [AppServices.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/di/AppServices.kt) · [NotificationAudience.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/domain/notification/NotificationAudience.kt)

## Agent message — 2026-10-25T02:27:56.973Z

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/owner_readers_review
Payload:
The null key needs to distinguish transient startup/read failures from a known unavailable account. As written, Ready → Failure clears the account’s `ViewModelStore`; recovery for the same account can lose draft or completion state.

Retaining that store alone also won’t preserve the `NavHost` back stack: `AppRoot` removes `AppNavigation` during Failure, which disposes its remembered controller and selected-primary state. Preserve saveable navigation state for the same scope through `Initializing` and `Failure`; clear both stores when the account is explicitly unavailable or a resolved scope changes. Add a regression test for Ready → Failure/Initializing → same Ready, plus checks that Unavailable and account changes release the old scope.

No files were changed or builds run.

Files: [AppNavigation.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/navigation/AppNavigation.kt) · [AppRoot.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/app/AppRoot.kt)

## Agent message — 2026-10-25T03:26:09.595Z

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/setup_ui_navigation_review
Payload:
- **P2 — Reverted availability choices are shown as pending changes.** [CampsiteAvailabilityStep.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/campsites/steps/CampsiteAvailabilityStep.kt) and [CampsiteReviewStep.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/campsites/steps/CampsiteReviewStep.kt) count requested values without comparing them to confirmed inventory. Tapping an unoffered date twice leaves `false` in the map, so the UI reports one night to withdraw even though availability is unchanged. The date also remains selected through `requested.keys` in `setupCalendarState`. Compare against a known baseline or present these as desired states rather than pending changes.

- **P2 — A photo selection can disappear silently during configuration change.** [CampsiteSetupRoute.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/campsites/CampsiteSetupRoute.kt) launches the content read in a composition-owned scope. If the route is disposed after the picker result arrives but before a slow read completes, cancellation prevents `selectPhoto` and is rethrown without showing a failure. Keep the read alive across configuration changes while canceling it on step or parent exit, or surface a recoverable result.

- **Test gap — Active wizard Back is untested.** [CampsiteSetupFlowTest.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/androidTest/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/campsites/CampsiteSetupFlowTest.kt) covers Cancel during editing, Back from the policy child, and Back from a load failure. It does not exercise the content-state BackHandler path: moving to the prior step or prompting to discard at the first step. Add a flow assertion for those behaviors and confirm dismissal preserves input.

Graph ownership, Create/Resume routing, policy return, disabled save/publish actions, and pending map messaging looked consistent with the M2a.2 scope. I did not edit files or run Gradle.

## Agent message — 2026-10-25T05:30:08.710Z

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/setup_controller_review
Payload:
Found one P2 issue: an unknown or failed current inventory read can still let a restored request be cleared as a no-op. The controller restores `known` values and compares against them without checking whether the current range loaded successfully ([CampsiteAvailabilityController.kt:69](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/campsites/CampsiteAvailabilityController.kt), [CampsiteAvailabilityController.kt:213](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/campsites/CampsiteAvailabilityController.kt)). If the comparator returns no differences, `save()` clears all requested dates and reports success without a write ([CampsiteAvailabilityController.kt:127](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/campsites/CampsiteAvailabilityController.kt)).

For example, a saved request asks to keep a date offered, based on an earlier `known=true` snapshot. The server value changes to false, then the restored range read fails. The stale comparison returns no differences, drops the request, and can allow parent completion. Require a successful current comparison before clearing a request as a no-op, while still allowing idempotent retries for explicitly unacknowledged dates.

I found no other concrete issues in listener ownership, write ordering, or cancellation. No builds or edits performed.

Payload:
Found one P2 regression: known values for offscreen months remain eligible for equality checks after their listener is cancelled. `changeMonth()` does not invalidate the month being left ([CampsiteAvailabilityController.kt:97](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/campsites/CampsiteAvailabilityController.kt)); the pending comparison uses any retained known value ([CampsiteAvailabilityController.kt:236](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/campsites/CampsiteAvailabilityController.kt)).

If a requested date’s server value changes while its month is offscreen, a stale baseline can make the request look equal. `save()` then clears it without calling `setOffered`. Invalidate known values when leaving a month or require a fresh read for each pending date before treating it as a no-op, and add an offscreen-month regression test. No builds or edits performed.

## Agent message — 2026-10-25T05:55:03.772Z

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/setup_controller_review
Payload:
Current code has two actionable findings; the new tests are present, but the fixes are pending.

- **P2 — Retry can silently downgrade Publish to draft.** In [CampsiteSetupViewModel.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/campsites/CampsiteSetupViewModel.kt), the early blocked-save branch records the failure without storing `action`. `retrySave()` later uses the stale `saveAction` at lines 87–90. A restored replacement-photo intent followed by `publish()`, photo discard/reselection, and `retrySave()` can therefore complete the draft without calling `campsites.publish()`. The new regression case at `CampsitePublicationTest.kt:304–320` covers this.

- **P2 — Confirmed unavailable targets can remain trapped behind publication uncertainty.** `recoverPublication()` treats a successful read returning an absent, removed, or foreign target as a read failure at lines 242–260, preserving the pending marker. On restoration, `load()` returns `Unavailable` at lines 659–667 but does not clear that marker; `requestExit()` then refuses to exit at lines 465–471. The new missing-target tests cover both paths.

I did not run builds or edit files.

Files: [CampsitePublicationTest.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/test/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/campsites/CampsitePublicationTest.kt)

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root

Payload:
**P2 — Back can abandon restored work before the parent read finishes.** In [AvailabilityViewModel.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/campsites/AvailabilityViewModel.kt), `requestExit()` checks only loaded `Content`. If the VM starts in `Loading` or reaches `Failure`, it sets `exitRequested` immediately, even when the saved state contains requested dates or a working selection. Since Back remains enabled in those states, navigating away can discard restored intent without confirmation. A regression test should restore requested dates or selection, hold/fail the parent read, call `requestExit()`, and verify exit waits for discard confirmation.

The parent validation, zone-based month, selection-to-request conversion, synchronous save guard, partial retry, cancellation, and missing-comparison paths otherwise look sound. I did not run builds.

One remaining recovery bug: [AvailabilityViewModel.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/campsites/AvailabilityViewModel.kt) leaves restored requested-date keys in `SavedStateHandle` when discard is confirmed before a controller exists. ## Agent message — 2026-10-25T06:23:51.948Z

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/setup_controller_review
Payload:
**P2 — Confirming discard before the parent loads leaves the requested intent in saved state.** In [AvailabilityViewModel.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/campsites/AvailabilityViewModel.kt), `confirmDiscard()` clears `availability.selection`, but clears requested dates only through `calendar?.discard()`. During Loading or Failure, `calendar` is null, so `availability.requested.dates` and `.offered` remain. If the destination is recreated before navigation finishes popping, that request can return and prompt again after the user confirmed discarding it. The new tests cover dismissing a restored request and confirming discard of a selection after failure, but not confirming a restored request before the parent resolves and checking that its saved intent is cleared.

The route correctly displays the separate prompt during Loading and Failure, and the late-read exit guard prevents constructing the controller after confirmed exit. No other blockers found.

If the user taps after the profile observer confirms hosting but before the root observer updates, [AppViewModel.selectWorkspace](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/app/AppViewModel.kt) silently rejects the request. The automatic retry is then suppressed, leaving an enabled “Start hosting” action with no failure feedback. Keep the action pending until the root confirms `Role.OWNER`, or expose whether selection was accepted.

- **Stale onboarding guidance:** [onboarding/README.md](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/onboarding/README.md) still describes the removed “Set up later” action and says the owner workspace opens Profile; the current flow uses a separate Profile “Start hosting” route and opens Campsites for owners. The activation and retry paths otherwise preserve the existing account, wait for confirmed capability, and have coverage for duplicate-write guards and root recovery.

## Agent message — 2026-10-25T12:48:53.425Z

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/setup_controller_review
Payload:
- **P2 — A known reservation can become `Unused` after refresh.** [LotteryDetailViewModel.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/lotteries/LotteryDetailViewModel.kt) rereads consumption on every foreground refresh while the opportunity is active. If the live reservation observer has emitted `null`, the state becomes `Unavailable(id)`; the next refresh calls `getForOpportunity`, and lines 213–215 map a null result to `Unused` without remembering the previously confirmed reservation ID. Reproduction: run the existing `missingObservedReservationNeverBecomesUnused` setup, then call `refreshTime()` and run the scheduler; the fake returns null and the state changes from `Unavailable` to `Unused`. Retain the known consuming ID across refresh/retry so confirmed absence stays `Unavailable`, or avoid rerunning the lookup for a known reservation. The same refresh also cancels a healthy live reservation observer and can replace `Reserved` with `Failure` on a transient server-read error.

## Codex — 2026-10-25T12:52:20.311Z

Review found one more edge case: refreshing a known reservation could incorrectly turn a missing read into an unused win. I added failing regressions, retained the confirmed reservation identity, and kept healthy live listeners across foreground refresh. All 48 focused reader and timing tests now pass.

## Agent message — 2026-10-25T12:53:08.079Z

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/setup_controller_review
Payload:
The known-consumption fix closes the finding. Once a reservation ID is confirmed, later absent reads produce `Unavailable(id)`, and healthy foreground refreshes leave its live observer intact. Failed-reader retries can reattach to that known ID without treating absence as `Unused`. Eligibility loss, parent retirement, and ViewModel clearing still cancel the work and reject late callbacks. The new regressions cover these paths; I found no further material issues.

## Agent message — 2026-10-25T13:42:03.291Z

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/setup_controller_review
Payload:
P2: The acknowledgement/observation handshake can leave Join or Leave permanently pending. In [LotteryDetailViewModel.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/lotteries/LotteryDetailViewModel.kt), confirmation checks only the *current* membership state after acknowledgement. If a matching state arrives while the write is held, then another device changes it before the acknowledgement, the match is lost and the action guard never clears. For example: Join is held; the stream emits `WAITING`, then `WITHDRAWN`; Join acknowledges. The UI remains stuck on `JOIN` even though `canJoin` is true. Leave has the symmetric case.

The tests cover observation-before-ack and ack-before-observation separately ([LotteryMembershipActionTest.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/test/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/lotteries/LotteryMembershipActionTest.kt)), but not a matching observation superseded before acknowledgement. Latch matching evidence per action, then settle once acknowledgement arrives; add regressions for both action directions.

## Agent message — 2026-10-25T15:48:45.638Z

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root
Sender: /root/setup_ui_navigation_review
Payload:
The failures share a missing focus precondition before system Back, but use different injection paths: StepScaffold, Discovery, and LotterySetup call Espresso `pressBack()`; Membership checks that the dialog root is displayed, then sends a real Back key. The harness’s focus poll only verifies the launcher before instrumentation starts, so it cannot ensure the later activity or dialog window has focus.

A bounded test-only wait for the active target window’s `hasWindowFocus()` before sending the same Back event would address that gap while preserving each test’s postconditions. Discovery also needs to wait for the actual IME-hidden state after `closeSoftKeyboard()`; that is a separate race. I couldn’t establish a deeper common cause without the failure stack or window dump, and I did not access the device or edit files.

Confirmed a lifecycle race in [CampsiteDetailRoute.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/campsites/CampsiteDetailRoute.kt): every section’s disposer sets choices visibility to false. Own the visibility effect only in the Lotteries child: set true on entry and false on that child’s disposal, keyed by the model. Preserve the failing assertions; they exercise the race.

## Agent message — 2026-10-25T18:41:41.443Z

Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: /root

- **P2 — A cancelled opportunity lookup can strand the active wizard in Loading.** In [BookingViewModel.kt](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/booking/BookingViewModel.kt), cancellation is rethrown and `finally` clears only `opportunityInFlight`; `opportunityAttempted` stays true while both `opportunityFailed` and `opportunityUnused` are false. Payload:
- **P2 — Re-entering Review can silently restore an invalidated price approval.** [enterReview](https://github.com/CMPUT301F26stderr/stderr-campsbridge/blob/5282afb1424a30bac10ec4d79296ca1037e7a31d/app/src/main/java/io/github/CMPUT301F26stderr/campsbridge/ui/booking/BookingViewModel.kt) always sets `reviewedTotalCents` to the current quote. Reproduction: reach Review at 13,000 cents, change the rate so approval is cleared, edit Guests, then press Next. Returning to Review sets the new 14,000-cent amount without `acknowledgeQuote`. This bypasses the explicit current-amount approval contract. Add a regression for that path and preserve the invalidated approval until the user explicitly acknowledges the current amount.

- **P2 — Date, month, and guest callbacks can mutate fields from the wrong step.** `changeMonth`, `selectCheckIn`, `selectCheckOut`, and `editGuests` only check `canEdit()`, which is true on any editable Content step; unlike terms and quote actions, they do not check `draft.step`. A stale callback after advancing can therefore change the stay or guest count while the wizard is on Review or Terms. Guard each action to its owning step and add a post-transition stale-callback test.
